that, in the opinion of MSD, are for the purpose of replacing lost income or maintaining or supplementing the person’s income and not for the purposes of paying or reimbursing specified costs of the person:
the amount of any accommodation benefit, basic grant, or independent circumstances grant payable to the person under the Student Allowances Regulations 1998:
the living-costs component of any student loan payable to the person.
Payments under subclause (3)﻿(g)—
include, without limitation, any payments payable to the person under the Telephone Costs Payment Programme established and approved by the Minister under section 124(1)﻿(d) of the Social Security Act 1964 (and continued under clause 21 of Schedule 1 of the Act); but
do not include any payments for childcare assistance or any payments made under any of the programmes specified in subclause (5).
The programmes referred to in subclause (4)﻿(b) (and approved and established under section 101(1) of the Act or, as the case requires, continued under clause 21 of Schedule 1 of the Act) are—
the Away from Home Allowance Programme:
the Home Help Programme:
the Social Rehabilitation Assistance Programme:
the Special Transfer Allowance Programme 2000:
the Families Package (Transitional Assistance) Programme:
the Employment and Work Readiness Assistance Programme:
the Income Support Package (Transitional Assistance) Programme:
the Abatement Threshold Increase (Transitional Assistance) Programme 2021:
the Benefit Rate Increase (Transitional Assistance) Programme 2021:
the Severe Weather Events TAA Programme.
In calculating the amount of a person’s chargeable income, MSD must disregard any deduction or reduction of the amount of any item of that income made or imposed by or under any statutory power, or under any deduction notice or attachment order or otherwise, on account of any debt, liability, sanction, penalty, child support payment, or student loan repayment.
Subclause (6) overrides subclauses (1) to (5).
70 Further limits on chargeable income: exempted payments and income
The items referred to in regulation 69 do not include payments and income exempted by Parts 2 and 3 of Schedule 8.
71 Allowable costs
For the purposes of this subpart and of section 98 of the Act, allowable costs—
means the regular essential expenses (as defined in subclause (2)) (if any) of the applicant and the applicant’s family reckoned on a weekly basis; and therefore
does not include an expense that is not an essential expense (as so defined).
In subclause (1), essential expense means an expense of a kind, and within the relevant limits (if any), specified in subclause (3), and that (unless the expense is one referred to in subclause (3)﻿(b), (c), (e)﻿(i), (h), or (i) and is in respect of an item acquired in accordance with subclause (4)), in MSD’s opinion,—
is essential for a person to pay or incur in order to meet the daily living needs of the person, of members of the person’s family, or of both; and
could not, when the expense or the liability for the expense was incurred, readily be avoided or varied.
The kinds of expenses and limits referred to in subclause (2) are as follows:
the person’s weekly qualifying accommodation costs (as defined in subclause (5)) less $34.13:
agreed period payments (as defined in subclause (6)), not exceeding the maximum weekly amount in column 3 of the following table, and made in connection with the acquisition of any of the items in column 1 of that table (within the maximum number of those items in column 2 of that table):
revolving credit payments (as defined in subclause (7)) for any of the items in column 1 of the table in paragraph (b) (being items acquired in accordance with subclause (4) and within the maximum numbers in column 2 of that table) and not exceeding (in terms of the rate of the payments, and either on a monthly or a weekly basis) the lesser of the maximum monthly payment (as defined in subclause (8)) and the maximum weekly payment in column 3 of that table:
costs for laundry or laundrette services, being costs incurred instead of regular payments for a washing machine or within a period during which the applicant could reasonably arrange to have repaired a washing machine available to the applicant, and not exceeding $31.80 per week:
disability costs (as defined in regulation 57),—
including any disability costs that are payments to which subclause (9) or (10) applies; but
excluding in all cases any costs of residential care services in respect of which temporary additional support must not be granted under section 96(2) of the Act:
for a person who is eligible for assistance under the Telephone Costs Payment Programme approved by the Minister under section 124(1)﻿(d) of the Social Security Act 1964 (and continued under clause 21 of Schedule 1 of the Act), and if paragraph (m) of this subclause does not apply, the weekly amount of telephone rental costs (as defined in subclause (11)) that were, immediately before 1 April 1999,—
claimed by the person; and
treated by MSD as an additional expense under section 69C(2A)﻿(a) of the Social Security Act 1964:
running costs, at a per kilometre rate set by MSD, for a motor vehicle where no suitable public transport is available for the purpose of transporting the applicant and the applicant’s spouse or partner to and from their places of employment and where the vehicle is essential for that purpose:
agreed period payments (as defined in subclause (6)) not exceeding $85.96 a week made in connection with the acquisition of a motor vehicle or other vehicle (for example, a bicycle)—
for the applicant and the applicant’s spouse or partner (if any) (whether or not either or both of them are beneficiaries), where no suitable public transport is available for the relevant purpose and the vehicle is essential—
for the purpose of transporting the applicant and the applicant’s spouse or partner to and from their places of employment; or
for the purpose of transporting the applicant or a member of the applicant’s family who is chronically ill or has a disability; or
for an applicant who is a beneficiary where—
there is no public transport reasonably available to the applicant; and
the contract or arrangement to acquire the vehicle was entered into before the applicant became a beneficiary:
revolving credit payments (as defined in subclause (7)) for a motor vehicle or other vehicle (for example, a bicycle) acquired in accordance with subclause (4) and where paragraph (h)﻿(i) or (ii)﻿(A) applies to the applicant and vehicle, and not exceeding (in terms of the rate of the payments, and either on a monthly or a weekly basis) the lesser of the maximum monthly payment (as defined in subclause (8)) and $85.96 per week:
the costs of public transport of the applicant and the applicant’s spouse or partner to and from their places of employment:
the net costs of essential childcare (that is, the gross costs of essential childcare, as defined in subclause (12), minus any childcare assistance payable under section 77 of the Act or by way of special assistance under section 101 of the Act):
essential expenses in respect of a child in the care of the applicant or the applicant’s spouse or partner for whom an orphan’s benefit or an unsupported child’s benefit is paid that are not able to be met from the total of that benefit and any disability allowance payable in respect of the child:
telephone rental costs (as defined in subclause (11)) for an applicant for whom, in the opinion of MSD, a telephone is a necessity—
because of the applicant’s special family circumstances (for example, to enable an applicant living in a rural location to be contacted by the school that the applicant’s children attend); or
to help to ensure the applicant’s personal safety or security (for example, a frail person living alone, or a separated person with a protection order against a spouse or partner); or
because of the applicant’s employment conditions (for example, an electrical worker on call 24 hours per day):
any child support required to be paid by a person for the week concerned under a formula assessment under the Child Support Act 1991 (regardless of whether, and, if so, how that support must under that Act be paid to, or otherwise dealt with to improve the position of, a receiving carer).
An item is acquired in accordance with this subclause for the purposes of subclauses (2), (3)﻿(c) and (i), and (10) if,—
when the item was acquired, neither the applicant nor the applicant’s spouse or partner (if any) was a beneficiary (whether or not either of them was a beneficiary, or both of them were beneficiaries, at any time before the item was acquired); and
at the time of the application for temporary additional support, the applicant or the applicant’s spouse or partner (if any) is a beneficiary, or both of them are beneficiaries, or neither of them is a beneficiary at that time but one or both of them became a beneficiary after the item was acquired.
For the purposes of subclause (3)﻿(a), a person’s weekly qualifying accommodation costs are that person’s weekly qualifying accommodation costs as defined in regulation 57 but—
do not include the following costs:
any costs of residential care services in respect of which temporary additional support must not be granted under section 96(2) of the Act; and
any severe weather events-related temporary accommodation costs of the person (even if those costs exceed the rate of, or are paid or incurred after the end of, or are otherwise not met or not to be met by, assistance under the Severe Weather Events TAA Programme that has been or may be granted to the person); and
include arrears except any that were incurred while the person or the person’s spouse or partner was in receipt of—
an accommodation benefit under the Student Allowances Regulations 1998; or
an accommodation supplement, a rent rebate allowance, or a tenure protection allowance under the Act; or
an income-related rent (as defined in section 2(1) of the Public and Community Housing Management Act 1992); or
assistance under the Severe Weather Events TAA Programme; and
include an emergency housing contribution (as that term is defined in regulation 3(1)); and
include a transitional housing contribution (as that term is defined in regulation 3(1)).
Agreed period payments, in relation to an item referred to in any of subclauses (3)﻿(b) and (h) and (9), means payments, during the agreed period, under a consumer credit contract or other arrangement that—
is evidenced in writing to the satisfaction of MSD; and
requires regular payments over an agreed period; and
was entered into to acquire the item or to repay debt incurred in acquiring the item; and
is not a revolving credit contract, an arrangement relating to an advance payment of all or part of 1 or more instalments of a benefit under section 347 of the Act, or an arrangement relating to a recoverable grant of assistance under section 101 of the Act.
Revolving credit payments, in relation to an item referred to in any of subclauses (3)﻿(c) and (i) and (10), means payments—
under a revolving credit contract (for example, a credit card contract) that requires regular payments and that was used to acquire the item; and
not exceeding (in total) the lesser of the cash price of the item and the amount owing under the contract at the time of the application for temporary additional support; and
for the period of 20 months after the acquisition of the item, if the amount owing under the contract at the time of the application for temporary additional support is equal to or greater than the cash price of the item; and
for whichever of the following periods ends first, if the amount owing under the contract at the time of the application for temporary additional support is less than the cash price of the item:
the period of 20 months after the acquisition of the item; and
the period in whole months that MSD estimates is necessary for that amount owing to be repaid fully at a rate of payment that is the maximum monthly payment (as defined in subclause (8)).
The maximum monthly payment, in relation to an item to which subclause (3)﻿(c) or (i) or (10) applies, means 5% of the cash price of the item.
The payments (referred to in subclause (3)﻿(e)﻿(i)) to which this subclause applies are agreed period payments (as defined in subclause (6)) not exceeding $31.80 per week made in connection with the acquisition of 1 clothes dryer for a person for whom the costs of acquiring a clothes dryer are additional costs arising from the person’s disability.
The payments (referred to in subclause (3)﻿(e)﻿(i)) to which this subclause applies are revolving credit payments (as defined in subclause (7)) for 1 clothes dryer, acquired in accordance with subclause (4), for a person for whom the costs of acquiring a clothes dryer are additional costs arising from the person’s disability, and not exceeding (in terms of the rate of the payments, and either on a monthly or a weekly basis) the lesser of the maximum monthly payment (as defined in subclause (8)) and $31.80 per week.
In subclause (3)﻿(f) and (m), telephone rental costs—
means the regular and ongoing costs of renting a basic telephone, or of telephone line rental, or of both, as well as any wiring maintenance charges paid; and therefore
does not include toll charges.
In subclause (3)﻿(k), gross costs of essential childcare, in relation to an applicant for temporary additional support, means the costs, up to a maximum of $6 per child per hour, of childcare—
that enables the applicant, or the applicant’s spouse or partner, or both, to take part in employment; or
that is required because the applicant is, or the applicant’s spouse or partner is, or both of them are, seriously disabled or seriously ill.
 Part 3 — Obligations
72 Guide to Part 3
Part 3 deals with the following topics:
subpart 1 (regulation 73) deals with when work-test obligations apply:
subpart 2 (regulations 75 to 80) deals with deferral of work-test obligations:
subpart 3 (regulations 81 to 89) provides for drug testing of beneficiaries:
subpart 4 (regulations 90 to 92) deals with social obligations in relation to dependent children:
subpart 5 (regulation 93) prescribes health practitioners for social obligations of a young person granted a young parent payment and relating to health care enrolment of a dependent child:
subpart 6 (regulation 94) prescribes health practitioners for obligations of a young person who is a spouse or partner of a beneficiary and relating to health care enrolment of a dependent child:
subpart 7 (regulations 95 and 96) provides for participation allowances:
subpart 8 (regulations 97 to 104) provides for exemptions from work-preparation or work-test obligations:
subpart 9 (regulations 105 to 107) sets out various exemptions from obligations under the Act of certain young people.
 Subpart 1 — When work-test obligations apply
73 Application of work-test obligations
This regulation prescribes days of the week for the purposes of, or under, sections 143(1)﻿(b) and 418(1)﻿(e) of the Act.
The work-test obligations set out in section 144(b) and (c) of the Act apply Monday to Sunday (inclusive).
The work-test obligation set out in section 146(1)﻿(d) of the Act applies Monday to Sunday (inclusive).
 Subpart 2 — Deferral of work-test obligations
74 Deferral of work-test obligations of person with dependent child under age of 1 year
75 Mandatory deferral of work-test obligations if person determined under section 141(1) of Act to lack capacity for part-time work
This regulation applies to a person who—
receives jobseeker support on the ground of health condition, injury, or disability; and
is the subject of a determination under section 141(1) of the Act that the person does not have the capacity for part-time work.
MSD must, after determining under section 141(1) of the Act that the person does not have capacity for part-time work (and whether or not the person has applied to defer that person’s work-test obligations), defer that person’s work-test obligations.
76 Deferral of work-test obligations on ground of later health condition, injury, or disability
This regulation applies to a person who MSD is satisfied—
receives jobseeker support; and
is subject to work-test obligations; and
has a health condition, an injury, or a disability that first arose or became apparent after the grant of jobseeker support; and
because of the health condition, injury, or disability, has no capacity for work or capacity only for work that is less than part-time work.
On the application of a person to whom this regulation applies or of its own initiative, MSD may defer all of that person’s work-test obligations.
77 Deferral of work-test obligations for young person required to receive youth services and on ground of undertaking study
This regulation applies to a person who is receiving jobseeker support if MSD is satisfied that—
the person is a young person who is required to receive youth services under section 165(2) of the Act; and
the person is subject to work-test obligations; and
the person’s undertaking of a full-time course that will give the person credits towards NCEA level 2 or an equivalent qualification would reduce the person’s risk of long-term welfare dependency (as defined in section 165(9) of the Act); and
the deferral of all of the person’s work-test obligations is necessary to enable the person to undertake that study.
On the application of a person to whom this regulation applies or of its own initiative, MSD may defer all of the person’s work-test obligations.
78 Deferral of work-test obligations for person receiving youth payment and on ground of undertaking study
This regulation applies to a person who is an applicant for jobseeker support if MSD is satisfied that—
the person has become eligible for jobseeker support while receiving a youth payment; and
the person is subject to work-test obligations; and
the person is a student in a full-time course of secondary instruction or tertiary education that will give the person credits towards NCEA level 2 or an equivalent or higher qualification that the person commenced or was undertaking while receiving the youth payment; and
the deferral of all of the person’s work-test obligations is necessary to enable the person to complete study towards that qualification.
On the application of a person to whom this regulation applies or of its own initiative, MSD may defer all of that person’s work-test obligations.
79 Procedure for deferral of work-test obligations
MSD may require an applicant for deferral of the applicant’s work-test obligations to verify particulars relevant to the application in the manner that MSD specifies, either generally or specifically.
A deferral under regulation 75, 76, 77, or 78 must be in writing and may be granted—
for a period set by MSD; or
on conditions set by MSD; or
both for a period, and on conditions, set by MSD.
Before deciding whether to defer a person’s work-test obligations under regulation 75 or 76, MSD must have regard to—
the certificate for the purpose of section 27 of the Act and any relevant report obtained under section 28 of the Act; and
any relevant work ability assessment under sections 115 to 119 of the Act.
80 MSD may review, and extend, vary, or revoke, deferral of work-test obligations
MSD may—
review a deferral granted under regulation 75, 76, 77, or 78; and
extend, vary, or revoke it.
 Subpart 3 — Drug testing
81 Interpretation
In this subpart, unless the context otherwise requires,—
AS/NZS 4308:2008 means the Australian/New Zealand Standard AS/NZS 4308:2008—Procedures for specimen collection and the detection and quantitation of drugs of abuse in urine
sample includes a sample that is or is referred to as a specimen
suitably qualified,—
for a person and a screening drug test, means that the person is qualified to provide that test because the person complies with (and is therefore a collector under) clause 1.3.14 of AS/NZS 4308:2008; and
for a person and recognised services for drug issue assessment and assistance (as defined in regulation 85(3)), means a health practitioner, or other person, who MSD is satisfied on reasonable grounds is suitably qualified to provide those services
testing, of a sample, includes collection, storage, handling, and dispatch of the sample.
In this subpart, unless the context otherwise requires, terms used and not defined in these regulations or in the Act, but defined in AS/NZS 4308:2008, have the meanings given to them by AS/NZS 4308:2008.
82 Evidential drug test: standard prescribed
This regulation prescribes a standard—
for the purposes of the definition (in section 152 of the Act) of evidential drug test; and
as contemplated by paragraph (c) of that definition.
The standard that this regulation prescribes for those purposes is the following provisions of AS/NZS 4308:2008 (if, or insofar as, the following provisions apply to evidentiary testing):
section 1:
section 2:
section 3:
section 5:
Appendices B to E.
Evidentiary testing, of a sample, means testing of the sample—
for evidential purposes (but not for screening purposes); and
that involves use of laboratory confirmatory procedures for the unequivocal identification and quantitation of controlled drugs.
83 Screening drug test: standard prescribed
This regulation prescribes a standard—
for the purposes of the definition (in section 152 of the Act) of screening drug test; and
as contemplated by paragraph (c)﻿(iii) of that definition.
The standard that this regulation prescribes for those purposes is the following provisions of AS/NZS 4308:2008 (if, or insofar as, the following provisions apply to screening testing, and subject to the modification in subclause (4)):
section 1:
section 2:
section 3:
section 4 (except the requirement in clause 4.10 that samples with results equal to, or greater than, the cut-off in Table 1 must be subjected to confirmatory testing):
Appendix A (if the screening testing is carried out not by way of laboratory screening but on site):
Appendices B to E.
Screening testing, of a sample, means testing of the sample—
for screening purposes (and evidential purposes); and
that involves use of a procedure for on-site or laboratory screening for controlled drugs; and
that does not involve laboratory confirmatory procedures for the unequivocal identification and quantitation of controlled drugs.
The modification referred to in subclause (2) is that the requirements in clauses 2.4 and 2.5, and in Appendix A, clause A3.2, paragraph (i), for dispatch of the sample to the laboratory do not apply if, after receiving from the potential employer or training provider advice that complies with subclause (5) on the effect of a waiver of those requirements, the beneficiary waives (in any manner) those requirements.
Advice from the potential employer or training provider on the effect of a waiver of those requirements must communicate that a waiver of those requirements—
results in the sample not being available for evidentiary testing (as defined in regulation 82(3)) for any purposes (for example, for the purposes of the beneficiary’s disputing under section 257 of the Act a sanction to be imposed under any of sections 234 to 238 of the Act); and
is therefore also a waiver of evidentiary testing (as so defined) for all purposes of the sample; and
results (in the absence of a good and sufficient reason described in section 250 of the Act) in a sanction under any of sections 234 to 238 of the Act being able to be imposed on the beneficiary based solely on the beneficiary’s having failed a screening drug test.
Nothing in this regulation limits or affects the right under section 149(1) of the Act of a candidate for employment or training to waive (in any manner) an evidential drug test requested by a potential employer or a training provider.
Nothing in this regulation limits or affects the right of a beneficiary to whom section 257 of the Act applies to dispute under that section a sanction to be imposed on the beneficiary (the beneficiary’s benefit being reduced, suspended, or cancelled), under any of sections 234 to 238 of the Act, based on the beneficiary’s having failed a screening drug test, by having the sample taken from the beneficiary for the screening drug test (if the beneficiary has not waived the requirement for dispatch of that sample to the laboratory) subjected to an evidential drug test.
84 Maximum reimbursement amounts prescribed
This regulation prescribes for the purposes of section 151 of the Act maximum amounts that MSD may reimburse an employer for the actual and reasonable costs of a screening drug test, an evidential drug test, or both, that the employer has (in accordance with section 147 of the Act) requested in respect of a work-tested beneficiary.
The maximum reimbursement amounts that this regulation prescribes are set out in Schedule 4.
85 Good and sufficient reason for specified failures to comply: ground specified
This regulation prescribes for the purposes of section 250(1)﻿(e) of the Act a ground on which a beneficiary may for the purposes of section 153 or 154(a) of the Act have a good and sufficient reason for either or both of the following specified failures:
not complying with a drug testing obligation under section 147 of the Act:
failing to apply for suitable employment that requires candidates to undertake drug tests.
The ground prescribed by this regulation is that—
the person has completed a screening process that has identified the person (based on information the person provided) as—
a user of (even if not dependent on, or addicted to) 1 or more controlled drugs; and
a person who requires or may require support in addressing the person’s use (including, without limitation, the causes of the person’s use) of 1 or more controlled drugs; and
the person has (in any manner) agreed to receive services for drug issue assessment and assistance for a period that—
is reasonable for that purpose; and
has been recommended by a suitably qualified provider of services of that kind; and
the period referred to in paragraph (b) has not expired.
Recognised services for drug issue assessment and assistance means services—
to assess any needs of the recipient for support in addressing the recipient’s use (including, without limitation, the causes of the recipient’s use) of 1 or more controlled drugs, give the recipient support of that kind, or both; and
that will be, or have been, provided by a suitably qualified provider of services of that kind.
86 Requirements for drug test
A compliant drug test of a person is—
a screening drug test of the person undertaken without any associated later evidential drug test of the person; or
an evidential drug test of the person if the person has failed any associated prior screening drug test under paragraph (a); or
an evidential drug test of the person undertaken without any associated prior screening drug test of the person.
87 Use of drug test result
This regulation prescribes, for the purposes of sections 150 and 431(1)﻿(b) of the Act, how MSD may use the result of a drug test undertaken under section 147 of the Act and disclosed to MSD under section 149(2) of the Act.
MSD may use the result for all or any of the following purposes:
for the purposes of all or any of sections 124 to 140, and subpart 2 of Part 5, of the Act:
for the purposes of a review or an appeal against a decision under all or any of the provisions specified in paragraph (a).
MSD must not use or communicate the result for any other purpose.
88 Costs of drug test
MSD may reimburse a potential employer for the costs of a drug test under section 147 of the Act requested by the employer if—
reimbursement of the costs is not prevented by section 151(2) of the Act (on the costs of an evidential drug test if the person waives the right to an evidential drug test in the situation specified in section 149(1) of the Act); and
the costs are the actual and reasonable costs of the test and do not exceed the prescribed maximum amount of reimbursement set out in Schedule 4 of these regulations; and
MSD has been provided with the result of the test; and
MSD is satisfied that the result relates to the person required to undergo the test; and
MSD is satisfied that the person required to undergo the test has failed the test.
89 Costs of drug test: supplementary provisions
Regulation 88 does not apply to the costs of a screening drug test if the person who undertook the test has later undertaken and passed an associated evidential drug test requested by the potential employer.
The amount that is reimbursed to a potential employer under regulation 88 is a debt due to the Crown from the person who undertook the drug test.
 Subpart 4 — Social obligations in relation to dependent children
90 Definitions relating to social obligations
For the purposes of sections 131 to 134 of the Act,—
approved early childhood education programme means—
an approved early childhood education programme (as defined in regulation 20 for the purposes of regulations made under section 424 of the Act); or
an early childhood education programme approved by MSD having regard to all relevant approval criteria (if any) prescribed for the purposes of this paragraph by the Minister under section 430 of the Act; or
a programme of early childhood education provided by a school that is currently designated under section 196(1) of the Education and Training Act 2020 as a distance school.
core check, for a child and a programme to which section 134(2) of the Act applies, means a check—
of the child’s health and well-being; and
done under, and identified in contract or funding arrangements as an essential part of, the programme; and
funded, directly or indirectly, through Vote Health
registered school has the meaning given by section 10(1) of the Education and Training Act 2020.
91 Prescribed target number of hours of attendance at approved early childhood education programme
This regulation prescribes a target number of hours of attendance at every approved early childhood education programme for the purposes of sections 131(1)﻿(b) and 132(1)﻿(a) of the Act.
The target number of hours that this regulation prescribes for those purposes is 15 hours per week.
92 Health care enrolment: prescribed health practitioners
A health practitioner who is either of the following, acting within their scope of practice, is prescribed for the purposes of section 134 of the Act (which relates to ensuring that a dependent child is enrolled with a primary health care provider who is a prescribed health practitioner who is a provider of primary health care):
a medical practitioner:
a nurse practitioner.
 Subpart 5 — Social obligations of young person granted young parent payment: health care enrolment of dependent child
93 Health care enrolment: prescribed health practitioners
A health practitioner who is either of the following, acting within their scope of practice, is prescribed for the purposes of section 164 of the Act (which relates to ensuring that a dependent child is enrolled with a primary health care provider who is a prescribed health practitioner who is a provider of primary health care):
a medical practitioner:
a nurse practitioner.
 Subpart 6 — Obligations of young person who is spouse or partner of beneficiary: health care enrolment of dependent child
94 Health care enrolment: prescribed health practitioners
A health practitioner who is either of the following, acting within their scope of practice, is prescribed for the purposes of section 167 of the Act (which relates to ensuring that a dependent child is enrolled with a primary health care provider who is a prescribed health practitioner who is a provider of primary health care):
a medical practitioner:
a nurse practitioner.
 Subpart 7 — Participation allowance
95 Participation allowance
MSD may pay any beneficiary a participation allowance in respect of the beneficiary’s participation under the Act in an activity of the kind described in the first column of Schedule 5.
The relevant rates of participation allowance are set out in the second column of Schedule 5.
96 Reimbursement portion of participation allowance
If a participation allowance includes a maximum amount for reimbursement of a beneficiary’s actual costs of participation in an activity, MSD must determine the amount to be paid to the beneficiary.
 Subpart 8 — Exemptions from work-preparation or work-test obligations
97 Procedure for grant of exemption
MSD may grant an exemption under section 158 of the Act—
on the application of the person concerned; or
on its own initiative.
MSD may require an applicant for an exemption under section 158 of the Act to verify particulars that are relevant to the application in the manner that MSD specifies.
Under section 158(2) of the Act, MSD—
may grant the exemption for a period set by MSD; and
may make the exemption subject to conditions set by MSD.
98 MSD may review, and extend, vary, or revoke, exemption
MSD may from time to time review an exemption granted under section 158 of the Act and may extend, vary, or revoke it.
99 Definition of special needs in relation to child
In this subpart and in regulation 107, unless the context otherwise requires, special needs, in relation to a child, means that the child has 1 or more of the following:
a physical or an intellectual disability:
a medical condition:
a chronic or recurring illness:
a learning or behavioural difficulty:
a need to adjust to a new placement as a foster child.
100 Exemption from work-preparation obligations
A person to whom section 124 or 125 of the Act applies may apply under section 158 of the Act to be exempted from some or all of the person’s obligations under section 124 or 125 of the Act, on a ground specified in subclause (2).
A person described in subclause (1) may be granted an exemption from some or all of the person’s obligations under section 124 or 125 of the Act on the grounds that—
the person has recently changed the person’s living circumstances because of family violence and needs time to adjust to the change; or
in the case of a person who falls within section 121 of the Act, the person needs time to adjust because—
the person’s spouse or partner has recently died; or
the person has recently separated from or ceased to live together with the person’s spouse or partner; or
the person’s de facto relationship has recently ended; or
the person is caring full-time for another person whose health condition, injury, or disability requires full-time care; or
the person has a health condition, an injury, or a disability that prevents the person from meeting the person’s obligations; or
the person is the principal caregiver of a dependent child or foster child with special needs (as defined in regulation 99)—
whose welfare would be unduly harmed if the person met the person’s obligations; and
for whom satisfactory alternative care arrangements are not available to, or are unable to be made by, the caregiver; or
the person’s capacity to meet the person’s obligations is limited because that person is subject to—
a sentence of supervision under the Sentencing Act 2002; or
release conditions (as that term is defined in section 4(1) of the Parole Act 2002); or
conditions of release on bail under any enactment; or
any other sentence or order imposed by a court.
101 Exemption from work-test obligations: all work-tested beneficiaries
Any beneficiary to whom the work test applies may apply under section 158 of the Act to be exempted from some or all of the beneficiary’s work-test obligations, on any ground specified in subclause (2).
A person described in subclause (1) may be granted an exemption from the work test on the ground that—
the person has recently changed the person’s living circumstances because of family violence and needs time to adjust to the change; or
the person’s benefit is being paid under an adopted reciprocity agreement, and the person resides in a country other than New Zealand; or
the person is serving a sentence of home detention under the Sentencing Act 2002; or
the person’s capacity to meet the obligations of the work test is limited because that person is subject to—
a sentence of supervision under the Sentencing Act 2002; or
release conditions (as that term is defined in section 4(1) of the Parole Act 2002); or
conditions of release on bail under any enactment; or
any other sentence or order imposed by a court; or
the person needs time to adjust because—
the person’s spouse or partner has recently died; or
the person has recently separated from or ceased to live together with the person’s spouse or partner; or
the person’s de facto relationship has recently ended.
In determining whether to grant an exemption from some or all of a person’s work-test obligations on the ground specified in subclause (2)﻿(a), MSD may consider, among other things, whether—
the person is living in temporary accommodation (for example, a refuge) while seeking more permanent accommodation:
the exemption is required to allow the person to help any children who are in the person’s care to adjust to the new family situation.
102 Exemption from work-test obligations: work-tested spouses or partners
A work-tested spouse or partner may apply under section 158 of the Act to be exempted from some or all of that person’s work-test obligations, on any ground specified in regulation 101 or in subclause (2).
A person described in subclause (1) may be granted an exemption from some or all of the person’s work-test obligations on the ground that—
it would be unreasonable to expect the person to undertake employment because—
the person is the principal caregiver of a dependent child or foster child with special needs (as defined in regulation 99)—
whose welfare would be unduly harmed if the person attended employment or training; and
for whom satisfactory alternative care arrangements are not available to, or are unable to be made by, the caregiver; or
the person would qualify for a supported living payment on the ground of restricted work capacity or total blindness under section 34 of the Act; or
the person is engaged in study of a kind approved by MSD and was so engaged when the person became subject to the work test; or
the person is caring on a full-time basis for a person with a health condition, an injury, or a disability of a kind that requires that person to be cared for full-time; or
the person is engaged in full-time study of a kind approved by MSD; or
both the following apply:
the person is actively engaged in teaching the person’s dependent child or foster child who is aged 5 years or is exempt from enrolment in school under an exemption granted under section 38 or 39 of the Education and Training Act 2020; and
it would be unreasonable to expect the child to attend school because of the family’s circumstances; or
the person has primary responsibility for the day-to-day care of a child, other than on a temporary basis, and the child—
is aged less than 3 years; and
is not a dependent child of that person; or
the person is—
at least 27 weeks pregnant; or
less than 27 weeks pregnant, if MSD is satisfied that the person is suffering from complications arising from the pregnancy; or
the person meets the criteria for jobseeker support on the health condition, injury, or disability ground in section 21(1)﻿(b) or 22(c) of the Act.
In determining whether to grant an exemption from some or all of a person’s work-test obligations on the ground that subclause (2)﻿(d)﻿(ii) applies to the person, MSD may consider, among other things, whether—
there are particular needs or reasons that prevent the child from attending school (for example, the child has special needs or has been excluded or expelled from school):
there are other mitigating circumstances involved (for example, home schooling is a reasonable option because the nearest school is a significant distance away).
103 Exemption from work-test obligations: work-tested sole parents receiving jobseeker support
A work-tested beneficiary who is a sole parent in receipt of jobseeker support may apply under section 158 of the Act to be exempted from some or all of that beneficiary’s work-test obligations on any of the following grounds:
any ground specified in regulation 101:
any ground specified in subclause (2).
A person described in subclause (1) may be granted an exemption from some or all of the person’s work-test obligations on the ground that—
it would be unreasonable to expect the person to undertake employment because the person is the principal caregiver of a dependent child or foster child with special needs (as defined in regulation 99)—
whose welfare would be unduly harmed if the person attended employment or training; and
for whom satisfactory alternative care arrangements are not available to, or unable to be made by, the caregiver; or
both of the following apply:
the person is actively engaged in teaching the person’s dependent child or foster child who is aged 5 years or is exempt from enrolment in school under an exemption granted under section 38 or 39 of the Education and Training Act 2020; and
it would be unreasonable to expect the child to attend school because of the family’s circumstances; or
the person has primary responsibility for the day-to-day care of a child, other than on a temporary basis, and the child—
is aged less than 3 years; and
is not a dependent child of that person.
In determining whether to grant an exemption from some or all of a person’s work-test obligations on the ground that subclause (2)﻿(b)﻿(ii) applies to the person, MSD may consider, among other things, whether—
there are particular needs or reasons that prevent the child from attending school (for example, the child has special needs or has been excluded or expelled from school):
there are other mitigating circumstances involved (for example, home schooling is a reasonable option because the nearest school is a significant distance away).
104 Exemption from work-test obligations: work-tested sole parent support beneficiaries
A work-tested sole parent support beneficiary may apply under section 158 of the Act to be exempted from some or all of that person’s work-test obligations, on any ground specified in regulation 101 or 103 or in subclause (2).
A person described in subclause (1) may be granted an exemption from some or all of that person’s work-test obligations on the ground that—
it would be unreasonable to expect the person to undertake employment because the person would qualify for a supported living payment on the ground of restricted work capacity or total blindness under section 34 of the Act; or
the person is—
at least 27 weeks pregnant; or
less than 27 weeks pregnant, if MSD is satisfied that the person is suffering from complications arising from the pregnancy; or
the person meets the criteria for jobseeker support on the health condition, injury, or disability ground in section 21(1)﻿(b) or 22(c) of the Act; or
the person is engaged in full-time study at a level higher than NCEA level 3 on the Qualifications and Credentials Framework developed by the New Zealand Qualifications Authority under section 436 of the Education and Training Act 2020; or
the person is engaged in study of a kind approved by MSD, and was so engaged when the person became subject to the work test; or
the person is engaged in full-time study of a kind approved by MSD; or
the person has primary responsibility for the day-to-day care of a child, other than on a temporary basis, and the child—
is aged less than 3 years; and
is not a dependent child of that person.
 Subpart 9 — Exemption from obligations under Act of certain young people
105 Exemption from obligations under section 162, 164, 166, or 167 of Act: youth support payment recipients and spouses and partners of specified beneficiaries
Any young person who is subject to obligations under section 162, 164, 166, or 167 of the Act may apply under section 158 of the Act to be exempted from some or all of those obligations on any ground specified in subclause (2).
A person referred to in subclause (1) may be granted an exemption from some or all of the person’s obligations under section 162, 164, 166, or 167 of the Act on the ground that—
the person has recently changed the person’s living circumstances because of family violence and needs time to adjust to the change; or
the person would qualify for a supported living payment on the ground of restricted work capacity or total blindness under section 34 of the Act; or
the person would meet the criteria (except for the person’s age) for jobseeker support on the health condition, injury, or disability ground in section 21(1)﻿(b) or 22(c) of the Act; or
the person is caring full-time for the person’s spouse or partner (who has a health condition, an injury, or a disability of a kind that requires that person to be cared for full-time); or
the person is serving a sentence of home detention under the Sentencing Act 2002; or
the person’s capacity to meet the person’s obligations under section 162, 164, 166, or 167 of the Act is limited because the person is subject to—
a sentence of supervision under the Sentencing Act 2002; or
release conditions (as that term is defined in section 4(1) of the Parole Act 2002); or
conditions of release on bail under any enactment; or
any other sentence or order imposed by a court.
106 Exemption from obligations under section 162 or 164 of Act: youth support payment recipients who have been in relationship
This regulation applies to any young person who receives—
a young parent payment; or
a youth payment.
Any young person to whom this regulation applies may apply under section 158 of the Act to be exempted from some or all of the person’s obligations under section 162 or 164 of the Act on the ground that the person needs time to adjust because—
the person’s spouse or partner has recently died; or
the person has recently separated from or ceased to live together with the person’s spouse or partner; or
the person’s de facto relationship has recently ended.
107 Exemption from obligations under section 162, 164, 166, or 167 of Act: young parent payment recipients, and young persons with dependent children who are spouses or partners of specified beneficiaries
This regulation applies to any young person who is subject to obligations under section 162, 164, 166, or 167 of the Act because—
the young person receives a young parent payment; or
section 167(1) of the Act applies.
Any young person to whom this regulation applies may apply under section 158 of the Act to be exempted from some or all of the person’s obligations under section 162, 164, 166, or 167 of the Act on the ground that—
the person is the principal caregiver of a dependent child or foster child with special needs (as defined in regulation 99)—
whose welfare would be unduly harmed if the person were required to comply with an obligation of the kind referred to in section 162, 164, 166, or 167 of the Act that requires full-time attendance; and
for whom satisfactory alternative care arrangements are not available to, or are unable to be made by, the caregiver; or
the person is aged 18 years and has primary responsibility for the day-to-day care of a foster child, other than temporarily, and the foster child is aged less than 12 months.
 Part 4 — Factors affecting benefits
108 Guide to Part 4
Part 4 deals with the following topics:
subpart 1 (regulations 109 to 112) deals with pre-benefit activities:
subpart 2 (regulations 113 to 117) deals with the effect of insurance recovery:
subpart 3 (regulations 118 to 123) deals with the effect of an entitlement to overseas pensions:
subpart 4 (regulations 124 to 136) deals with overseas pension deduction:
subpart 6 (regulations 139 to 153) deals with factors affecting benefits: absence from New Zealand:
subpart 7 (regulations 154 to 162) deals with beneficiaries excluded from provisions about the effect of a warrant for a beneficiary’s arrest.
 Subpart 1 — Factors affecting benefits: pre-benefit activities
109 Who can MSD require to undertake activities?
Regulation 110 applies to a person who contacts MSD requesting financial assistance under the Act, if MSD considers that the appropriate financial assistance would be a benefit that is—
jobseeker support; or
sole parent support; or
an emergency benefit.
Regulation 110 also applies to the spouse or partner of a person who contacts MSD requesting financial assistance under the Act, if the spouse or partner is not, in the spouse’s or partner’s own right, receiving or applying for a main benefit under the Act (as defined in Schedule 2 of the Act), New Zealand superannuation, or a veteran’s pension, and MSD considers that the appropriate financial assistance would be a benefit that is—
jobseeker support; or
a supported living payment on the ground of restricted work capacity or total blindness; or
an emergency benefit.
110 Requirements related to activities
MSD may, for the purpose stated in section 3(a)﻿(ii) of the Act, require a person to whom this section applies (under regulation 109(1) or (2)), unless MSD has determined that the person does not have the capacity to seek, undertake, and be available for part-time work, to do any or all of the following:
undertake 1 or more stated pre-benefit activities:
at a time before the benefit to which regulation 109(1) or (2) applies commences, attend and participate in any interview for an opportunity of suitable employment to which the person is referred by MSD:
at a time before the benefit to which regulation 109(1) or (2) applies commences, accept any offer of suitable employment (whether or not the offer results from an interview of the kind described in paragraph (b)).
A requirement under subclause (1) may be oral or written and, in the case only of a requirement under subclause (1)﻿(a), may be revoked orally or in writing in accordance with subclause (4).
A pre-benefit activity stated under subclause (1)﻿(a) must be available for the person to undertake not later than 10 working days after the date of first contact.
MSD may revoke a requirement that the person undertake a pre-benefit activity under subclause (1)﻿(a) if, and only if, MSD considers the requirement is no longer reasonable.
Pre-benefit activity, in this regulation and regulations 111 and 112 and section 184 of the Act, means an activity that is not an activity in the community (as defined in Schedule 2 of the Act) but is all or any of the following:
reporting on the progress of job-search activities:
completing a curriculum vitae and providing it to MSD:
attending and participating in an employment-related seminar provided by MSD or by any other person approved by MSD for the purpose:
attending and participating in employment-related training approved by MSD for the purpose:
attending and participating in any interview with an MSD employee:
completing any self-assessment or planning required by MSD.
Suitable employment, for the purposes of subclause (1),—
for a person who has a dependent child aged under 14 years, and to whom this regulation applies by virtue of regulation 109(1)﻿(b) or (2), means employment that would be suitable employment within the meaning of Schedule 2 of the Act if the person were a part-time work-tested beneficiary:
for a person for whom MSD considers that the appropriate financial assistance under the Act would be jobseeker support on the ground of health condition, injury, or disability, means employment that would be suitable employment within the meaning of Schedule 2 of the Act if the person were a part-time work-tested beneficiary:
for any other person, means employment that would be suitable employment within the meaning of Schedule 2 of the Act if the person were a work-tested beneficiary.
111 Non-compliance by applicant
In this regulation,—
applicant means a person to whom regulation 109(1) applies
specified benefit means the benefit to which regulation 109(1) applies.
MSD is not required under section 298(1) of the Act to inquire into a claim for the specified benefit made by the applicant unless satisfied that the applicant has undertaken any required pre-benefit activities.
Subclause (2) applies whether or not the applicant has completed the form of application for the specified benefit, and (under section 298(2)﻿(c) of the Act) overrides section 298(1) of the Act.
If the applicant fails to undertake any required pre-benefit activities within 20 working days after the date of first contact, any application for the specified benefit the applicant has made lapses.
Subclause (4) overrides regulation 168 (date application received for when benefit commences).
If MSD considers that the applicant has, without a good and sufficient reason, failed to comply with a requirement under regulation 110(1)﻿(b) or (c) (which relate to interviews for, or offers of, suitable employment), MSD must, as the case requires,—
refuse to grant the applicant the specified benefit; or
cancel any grant of the specified benefit already made to the applicant.
112 Non-compliance by applicant’s spouse or partner
In this regulation,—
applicant means a person who contacts MSD in accordance with regulation 109(1)
applicant’s spouse or partner means an applicant’s spouse or partner to whom regulation 109(2) applies
specified benefit means the benefit to which regulation 109(2) applies.
This subclause applies to an applicant’s spouse or partner if MSD considers that the applicant’s spouse or partner—
has failed to undertake a required pre-benefit activity within 20 working days after the date of the applicant’s first contact; or
has failed without a good and sufficient reason to comply with a requirement under regulation 110(1)﻿(b) or (c).
If subclause (2) applies to an applicant’s spouse or partner, the applicant receives the following (if all or any of them is granted) at the following rate or rates only:
the specified benefit—half of the rate of that benefit payable to a beneficiary—
who is in a relationship; and
whose spouse or partner is not granted a benefit in the spouse’s or partner’s own right:
the accommodation supplement, temporary additional support, or both (if any), to which the applicant and the applicant’s spouse or partner would apart from this subclause be entitled—half of the otherwise applicable rate or rates.
Subclause (3) overrides other regulations of these regulations but does not preclude a review under subpart 3 of Part 6 of the Act (review of entitlement to, or rate of, benefit granted).
 Subpart 2 — Factors affecting benefits: insurance recovery
113 Interpretation
In regulations 114 to 117,—
benefit includes special assistance under a welfare programme approved under section 101 of the Act
debt insurance payment, in relation to a person (P), includes any payment made or provided on the occurrence of a contingency under a contract of insurance or by reason of P’s membership of any society, organisation, or body (whether incorporated or not)—
for the purpose of enabling P to make payments that P is liable to make on account of any debt or liability; and
that is not for any reason treated as being the income of P under these regulations.
114 Income-related insurance payment
This regulation applies when a person (P) applies for a benefit and is, or may be, entitled or eligible to receive, or receive the equivalent in value of, an income-related insurance payment (the payment).
If MSD grants the benefit, MSD may make the benefit or rate of benefit subject to the condition that all or part of the benefit as MSD requires must be repaid to MSD if the payment is made to—
P; or
another person on P’s behalf or for P’s benefit; or
a member of P’s family.
The amount repayable to MSD under subclause (2)—
constitutes a charge on the payment referred to in subclause (1); and
is a debt due to the Crown from the person to whom the payment is made; and
is subject to recovery under section 362(1) of the Act (MSD’s duty to recover debts) from—
P; or
the person to whom the payment is made; or
the person liable to make the payment.
115 Debt insurance or health or disability insurance payment
This regulation applies when a person (P) applies for a benefit specified in subclause (2) and is, or may be, entitled or eligible to receive, or receive the equivalent in value of, an insurance payment (the payment) that is 1 of the following:
a debt insurance payment:
a health insurance payment:
a disability insurance payment.
The benefits referred to in subclause (1) are benefits of a kind referred to in any of the following sections of the Act:
section 65 (accommodation supplement):
section 85 (disability allowance):
section 96 (temporary additional support):
section 101 (special assistance under an approved welfare programme).
If MSD grants the benefit, MSD may make the benefit or rate of benefit subject to the condition that all or part of the benefit as MSD requires must be repaid to MSD if the payment is made to—
P; or
another person on P’s behalf or for P’s benefit; or
a member of P’s family.
The amount repayable to MSD under subclause (3)—
constitutes a charge on the payment referred to in subclause (1); and
is a debt due to the Crown from the person to whom the payment is made; and
is subject to recovery under section 362(1) (MSD’s duty to recover debts) of the Act by MSD from—
P; or
the person to whom the payment is made; or
the person liable to make the payment.
116 Calculation of insurance payment
For the purposes of these regulations, the amount of an income-related insurance payment or a debt insurance payment or a health or disability insurance payment must, to the extent that MSD determines, be reduced by the amount of any costs incurred by an applicant for a benefit or a beneficiary in obtaining receipt of that payment.
117 Effect on liabilities of insurance payments
This subpart does not limit or affect Part 5 of Schedule 3 of the Act.
 Subpart 3 — Factors affecting benefits: entitlement to overseas pensions
118 Interpretation
In regulations 119 to 123,—
benefit means a benefit under NZ benefits legislation
overseas pensioner means a person who receives an overseas pension
person affected by the receipt of an overseas pension has the same meaning as in section 188 of the Act.
119 Benefit of person affected by receipt of overseas pension is reduced by amount of overseas pension
The rate of the benefit or benefits that would otherwise be payable under NZ benefits legislation (as defined in section 187 of the Act) to a person affected by the receipt of an overseas pension must be reduced under section 189(1) of the Act by the amount of the overseas pension to be deducted, as that amount is determined by MSD under this subpart and subpart 4.
120 Benefits of overseas pensioner not to be reduced for overseas pension in nature of compensation for injury or death, etc
MSD may determine that an overseas pension is in the nature of, and is paid for similar purposes as,—
compensation for injury or death for which payment could be made under the Accident Compensation Act 2001 if the injury or death had occurred in New Zealand after the commencement of that Act; or
a war disablement pension or a disablement pension or allowance that—
is granted under the Veterans’ Support Act 2014, excluding Part 6 of that Act; and
is of a type that would not affect any recipient’s entitlement to a benefit in accordance with section 200 of the Act (exceptions to rule that veteran’s entitlement excludes any other benefit); or
a disability allowance granted under the Act.
No reduction may be made under regulation 119 for an overseas pension that MSD has determined falls within subclause (1)﻿(a), (b), or (c).
121 Date that determination takes effect
As part of the determination under regulation 119, MSD may fix the date on which a determination that an overseas pension is a qualifying overseas pension takes effect.
The date may be before, on, or after the determination.
122 MSD may pay overseas pensioner benefit in return for amount of overseas pension
This regulation applies in the case of a person affected by the receipt of an overseas pension.
MSD and the overseas pensioner in question may (in accordance with and subject to the conditions set out in subclause (3)) enter into an arrangement under which—
MSD pays the overseas pensioner, in line with the arrangement, the rate of a benefit or benefits under the NZ benefits legislation; and
MSD receives from the overseas pensioner an amount that is equivalent to the amount of the overseas pension that the overseas pensioner receives.
The conditions are that—
the overseas pensioner has agreed to make the arrangement; and
the arrangement must comply with this subpart and subpart 4; and
the overseas pensioner must not have voluntarily cancelled a previous such arrangement with MSD; and
MSD has not previously ceased to make payments under subclause (2)﻿(a) because of the overseas pensioner’s failure to comply with the arrangement.
123 Arrangements with overseas pensioners: administration
MSD may from time to time, on behalf of the Crown, enter into contracts with 1 or more registered banks for the purpose of implementing arrangements under regulation 122.
Money in a bank account maintained in accordance with these regulations to implement an arrangement under regulation 122 must not be—
attached or taken in execution under any order or process of any court:
made the subject of any set-off, charge, or assignment:
claimed or deducted under any notice or other instrument issued under any enactment (other than this subpart).
If the benefit under an arrangement under regulation 122 is New Zealand superannuation, MSD must pay any amount received from the overseas pensioner under the arrangement into the New Zealand Superannuation Fund established under the New Zealand Superannuation and Retirement Income Act 2001, in accordance with arrangements made from time to time by MSD with the Guardians of New Zealand Superannuation.
For the purposes of the Act, a payment to an overseas pensioner under an arrangement under regulation 122 is, for the period to which the payment relates, treated as payment of—
the benefit or benefits that are payable to the overseas pensioner in that period under the NZ benefits legislation, at the rate at which that benefit or those benefits are so payable but reduced under regulation 119; and
the amount of the overseas pension that the overseas pensioner is entitled to receive or has received in that period.
 Subpart 4 — Overseas pension deduction
124 Interpretation
In this subpart, unless the context otherwise requires,—
average, in relation to a calculation period, includes (but is not limited to) the case in which there is only 1 exchange rate (in which case the average is that exchange rate)
business day means a day on which a nominated bank is open for business
calculation period means a period—
beginning on the commencement of the 16th day of one month; and
ending with the close of the 15th day of the following month
calculation rate has the meaning given to it by regulation 126
cross rate means a currency exchange rate between the New Zealand dollar and the currency of a country paying an overseas pension computed from time to time, where applicable, by pricing the New Zealand dollar and the currency of that country against the United States of America dollar
figure, in relation to the calculation rate, means an average of the cross rates at which the nominated bank would purchase an overseas currency for settlement by telegraphic transfer as at the opening of business on each business day during the calculation period immediately before the payment month for which the calculation is made
nominated bank means a bank for the time being nominated by MSD
notification deadline means—
the close of the fifth day after the end of the calculation period; or
if that fifth day is not a working day, the close of the next working day
payment month, in relation to an instalment of a benefit, means the calendar month in which the instalment is payable (under section 338 of the Act).
125 Reduction formula
If section 189(1) of the Act requires a benefit to be reduced,—
each instalment of the benefit payable in the payment month immediately following the end of a calculation period must be reduced; and
the amount per week by which each instalment is reduced must be calculated according to the following formula:
where—
is the rate per week of the overseas pension, in the currency of the country paying the pension payable to the overseas pensioner during the calculation period; and
is the calculation rate determined under regulation 126; and
is the bank fee determined under regulation 127.
If the benefit is payable by an instalment of 2 or more weeks’ benefit, the amount by which each instalment is reduced is the amount calculated according to the formula in subclause (1)﻿(b) multiplied by the appropriate number of weeks.
Unless provided otherwise in any adopted reciprocity agreement, a reference in this regulation to an instalment of a benefit or to an overseas pension is a reference to that instalment or pension before the deduction of income tax (if any).
126 Calculation rate
For the purposes of the formula set out in regulation 125(1)﻿(b), the calculation rate is,—
in the case where subclause (2) does not apply, the figure for the currency of the country paying the overseas pension that the nominated bank notifies to MSD; or
in the case where subclause (2) applies, the more favourable to the overseas pensioner of the following:
the figure specified in paragraph (a); or
the currency exchange rate notified under subclause (2).
This subclause applies if a country (or a bank on its behalf) paying the overseas pension—
notifies MSD or provides public notice of the currency exchange rate used to convert that country’s overseas pensions to New Zealand currency for payment of that country’s overseas pensions in New Zealand, in which case the currency exchange rate for the purposes of subclause (1)﻿(b)﻿(ii) is that rate:
notifies more than 1 exchange rate used to convert that country’s overseas pensions to New Zealand currency, in which case the currency exchange rate for the purposes of subclause (1)﻿(b)﻿(ii) is the average of those rates:
notifies an average currency exchange rate used to convert that country’s overseas pensions to New Zealand currency, in which case the currency exchange rate for the purposes of subclause (1)﻿(b)﻿(ii) is that average rate.
Subclause (2) does not apply unless a currency exchange rate is notified to MSD or publicly by the country (or a bank on its behalf) paying the overseas pension before the notification deadline that is immediately before the payment month for which the calculation is made.
127 Bank fee
For the purposes of the formula set out in regulation 125(1)﻿(b), the bank fee—
is the amount in New Zealand currency (not exceeding $40) specified by MSD to compensate an overseas pensioner for any of the following fees that MSD is satisfied that the overseas pensioner has incurred:
a fee charged by an overseas country’s bank to convert the currency of the overseas country into New Zealand currency for the purpose of payment of an overseas pension into New Zealand:
a fee charged by the overseas pensioner’s New Zealand bank to convert an overseas pension into New Zealand currency:
if the overseas country will not or cannot pay an overseas pension to a New Zealand bank account, an amount charged by an overseas bank to convert the currency of the overseas country into New Zealand currency for the purpose of payment of the pension into the pensioner’s New Zealand bank account:
a fee charged by an overseas country’s bank to load an overseas pension onto an electronic cash, credit, or debit card; but
does not include—
any fee charged by an overseas bank to receive an overseas pension if that pension is not transferred by that bank to a New Zealand bank; or
any fee associated with the overseas pensioner accessing an overseas pension loaded onto an electronic cash, credit, or debit card; or
any margin included in any currency exchange rate used to convert an overseas currency into New Zealand currency, whether directly or indirectly.
128 Determination conclusive
A determination made by MSD under section 189(1) of the Act and in accordance with these regulations is conclusive.
This regulation does not limit anything in sections 304 to 310 of the Act.
 Alternative arrangements for pensions
129 Interpretation
In regulations 130 to 136, unless the context otherwise requires,—
alternative arrangement has the meaning given to it by regulation 130
competent authority means the competent authority of the country paying the overseas pension
eligible overseas pensioner means an overseas pensioner to whom MSD may make an offer under regulation 133
nominated bank—
means a bank with which MSD has, for the time being, entered into an agreement under regulation 132(1); and
where MSD has entered into such agreements with different banks in respect of overseas pensions payable by different countries, under regulation 132(3), means, in relation to an overseas pensioner, the bank with which MSD has entered into an agreement in respect of the country paying the overseas pensioner’s overseas pension
operate, in relation to a special bank account, includes—
to deposit money into the account:
to withdraw money from the account, or to draw on the account in any other way:
to authorise another person to do any action referred to in paragraph (a) or (b)
special bank account has the meaning given to it by regulation 131.
130 Meaning of alternative arrangement
In these regulations, alternative arrangement means an arrangement, made under section 190 of the Act by MSD with an eligible overseas pensioner (as that term is defined in regulation 129), that includes—
the condition that no payment may be made under section 190 of the Act before 26 November 2018 (or, for an arrangement saved by clause 30(2) of Schedule 1 of the Act, that no payment may be made under section 70(3)﻿(b) of the Social Security Act 1964 before 1 April 1997); and
the condition that the disposition by the overseas pensioner of the overseas pensioner’s overseas pension to MSD is made in consideration of MSD making payments to the overseas pensioner under section 190 of the Act; and
the conditions set out in subclause (2).
The conditions referred to in subclause (1)﻿(c) are that the overseas pensioner—
complies with any obligation imposed on the overseas pensioner by these regulations; and
signs a written authority to the nominated bank to—
open and administer a special bank account in the overseas pensioner’s name; and
operate the account for the purposes for which it may be operated; and
on the occurrence of any of the circumstances set out in subclause (3), transfer any money in the account to MSD and close the account; and
signs a written authority to the competent authority to pay the overseas pension into the overseas pensioner’s special bank account; and
must not revoke an authority given under paragraph (b) or (c) unless the overseas pensioner closes the special bank account with effect from the date on which the revocation of the authority takes effect; and
must not operate the overseas pensioner’s special bank account for any purpose for which it may not be operated; and
agrees that MSD, the competent authority, and the nominated bank may disclose to one another—
information about the overseas pensioner’s special bank account; and
information about payments of the overseas pension made to the special bank account,—
if the disclosure is necessary to facilitate the administration of the overseas pensioner’s alternative arrangement or special bank account.
The circumstances referred to in subclause (2)﻿(b)﻿(iii) are that—
the nominated bank receives notice from MSD of the cessation of the alternative arrangement with the overseas pensioner; or
the nominated bank receives notice from MSD of the termination of the alternative arrangement with the overseas pensioner; or
the overseas pensioner attempts to operate the overseas pensioner’s special bank account for a purpose for which it may not be operated; or
the nominated bank is notified, by means on which it is reasonable to rely, that—
the overseas pensioner has ceased to reside in New Zealand; or
the overseas pensioner has died.
131 Meaning of special bank account
In these regulations, the term special bank account means a bank account that is opened and administered by the nominated bank in the name of an overseas pensioner in respect of a pension or pensions received from a country listed in regulation 133(b).
The purposes for which a special bank account may be operated are—
to receive payments of the overseas pensioner’s overseas pension or pensions from the competent authority of a country listed in regulation 133(b); and
to transfer to MSD, from time to time, amounts equivalent to the amounts of those payments of the overseas pension or pensions.
No special bank account may—
be opened and administered in the name of more than 1 overseas pensioner; or
be operated for any purpose other than a purpose referred to in subclause (2); or
be operated in respect of more than 1 country listed in regulation 133(b).
132 Nominated bank
MSD must from time to time enter into an agreement with a bank, whether by way of contract or otherwise, specifying terms and conditions in respect of the opening, administration, and operation of special bank accounts.
The terms and conditions of any agreement entered into under subclause (1) may include, without limitation, terms and conditions relating to—
the obligations of the bank not to—
open or administer any special bank account in the name of more than 1 overseas pensioner; or
allow any special bank account to be operated for any purpose for which it may not be operated:
the frequency with which the nominated bank must transfer to MSD amounts equivalent to amounts of payments of overseas pensioners’ overseas pensions:
the disclosure of information by MSD, the competent authority, and the nominated bank to one another about—
overseas pensioners’ special bank accounts; and
payments of overseas pensions made to special bank accounts,—
if the disclosure is necessary to facilitate the administration of overseas pensioners’ alternative arrangements or special bank accounts:
the obligation of the bank to notify MSD of the closure of any special bank account and of the reason for the closure:
the transfer to MSD of any money in a special bank account that is closed for any reason other than the bankruptcy of the overseas pensioner.
MSD may enter into agreements under subclause (1) with different banks in respect of overseas pensions payable by different countries.
133 Category of eligible overseas pensioners
MSD may offer to make an alternative arrangement with any overseas pensioner who—
is not an undischarged bankrupt; and
is entitled to receive or receives an overseas pension paid by, or on behalf of, any of the following:
the Government of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland:
the Government of the Commonwealth of Australia:
the Government of the Kingdom of the Netherlands:
the Government of Ireland:
the States of Jersey:
the States of Guernsey.
134 Making of alternative arrangement
An overseas pensioner who wishes to accept an offer made under regulation 133 must—
give MSD written notice of the overseas pensioner’s acceptance; and
attach to that written notice the authorities signed under regulation 130(2)﻿(b) and (c).
135 Cessation of alternative arrangement
An alternative arrangement ceases if—
the nominated bank closes the special bank account on the occurrence of any of the circumstances set out in regulation 130(3)﻿(c) or (d); or
the overseas pensioner gives MSD written notice that the alternative arrangement is to cease; or
MSD determines that the overseas pensioner either never has been or is no longer—
entitled to a benefit; or
an overseas pensioner to whom regulation 133 applies; or
the overseas pensioner ceases to reside in New Zealand; or
the overseas pensioner is adjudicated bankrupt; or
the overseas pensioner dies.
MSD must give notice of the cessation of any alternative arrangement—
to the competent authority, where any of paragraphs (a) to (f) of subclause (1) applies; and
to the nominated bank, where any of paragraphs (b) to (f) of subclause (1) applies.
The cessation of an alternative arrangement under subclause (1) takes effect immediately after—
the nominated bank transfers any money in the special bank account to MSD (or, where the overseas pensioner has been adjudicated bankrupt, to the Official Assignee) and closes the special bank account; and
MSD makes (except where the overseas pensioner has been adjudicated bankrupt) the corresponding payment under section 70(3)﻿(b) of the Social Security 1964 or section 190 of the Act.
136 Termination of alternative arrangement
MSD may terminate an alternative arrangement, with effect from a date determined by MSD, if the overseas pensioner fails to comply with any condition set out in regulation 130(2).
MSD must give notice of the termination of any alternative arrangement—
to the competent authority; and
to the nominated bank.
 Subpart 5 — Factors affecting benefits: failure to assist child support
137 Reduction amount
138 Additional reduction amount
 Subpart 6 — Factors affecting benefits: absence from New Zealand
139 Interpretation
In this subpart, unless the context otherwise requires,—
4-week rule means the rule set out in regulation 142
benefit subject to obligations rule means the rule set out in regulation 144
family member, in relation to a beneficiary, includes a person who is treated by the beneficiary as, and acknowledged by the beneficiary to be, a member of the beneficiary’s family
general rule means the rule set out in regulation 140(1)
medical exception means the exception set out in regulation 150
natural disaster or adverse event means a fire, flood, drought, or other emergency involving damage or danger to life or property
other enactments exception means the exception set out in regulation 153
part-time work-tested beneficiary has the meaning set out in paragraph (a) of the definition of that term in Schedule 2 of the Act
Special Olympic or Paralympic Games exception means the exception set out in regulation 152
vocational training exception means the exception set out in regulation 151.
In this subpart, a significant event, in relation to a beneficiary’s family member, includes the following events:
serious illness or serious injury or imminent death:
any of the following connected to the family member’s death:
a funeral (however called) or unveiling:
if the beneficiary has missed the family member’s funeral, a visit to pay respects to a family member of the deceased:
attending to legal matters in relation to the deceased’s affairs:
a wedding or civil union:
the birth of a child:
a court case in which a family member is a party or witness.
In regulations 142, 143, and 144, a benefit is a qualifying benefit for the purposes of the 4-week rule if it is not one of the following benefits:
sole parent support for a work-tested beneficiary:
supported living payment on the ground of restricted work capacity or total blindness for a work-tested spouse or partner:
jobseeker support (other than jobseeker support for a spouse or partner who is not a work-tested spouse or partner):
an emergency benefit:
any benefit that, but for the 1 or more absences in question, would be payable to a beneficiary who is subject to 1 or more work-preparation obligations required by MSD under section 125(d) of the Act:
a youth payment:
a young parent payment.
