jobseeker support; and
the other assistance specified in regulation 190N(3)﻿(b).
190M Person intending to reapply contacts MSD before expiry date
This regulation applies if a person intending to reapply for jobseeker support, or any included spouse or partner, contacts MSD before the expiry date for the person’s jobseeker support.
MSD may set a time (being a time within 10 working days starting on that expiry date) before which the requirements of regulation 190J(1)﻿(a) to (c) are to be completed (by the person and by any included spouse or partner).
If those requirements are completed (by the person and by any included spouse or partner) before that time, MSD may regrant the person’s jobseeker support if MSD is satisfied that—
the person meets the eligibility criteria for that benefit in section 20 of the Act; and
on and after that expiry date, and unless exceptional circumstances prevented the person from doing so, the person continued to meet the eligibility criteria for that benefit in section 20 of the Act.
If regranted, that benefit commences on that expiry date.
If regranted, that benefit is not payable to the person for the relevant period as well as the conditional payment to the person for the relevant period of the following that are payable under regulation 190N:
jobseeker support; and
the other assistance specified in regulation 190N(3)﻿(b).
190N Conditional payment of jobseeker support and other assistance
This regulation applies only if, in relation to a person,—
the requirements of regulation 190J(1)﻿(a) to (c) are not completed (by the person and by any included spouse or partner) within the period allowed to complete the requirements; and
the person’s jobseeker support is not regranted, and does not commence on that expiry date, under regulation 190K(2) and (3); and
those requirements may be completed in the relevant period.
The relevant period, for the purposes of this regulation, is—
the 20-working-day period mentioned in regulation 190L; or
if regulation 190M applies, the period that—
starts when the person, or any included spouse or partner, contacts MSD; and
ends at the time MSD sets under regulation 190M before which the requirements of regulation 190J(1)﻿(a) to (c) are to be completed.
MSD must, for the relevant period, grant and pay the person—
jobseeker support; and
all other assistance under this Act—
payable to the person immediately before the expiry date for the person’s jobseeker support; and
that would have continued to be payable to the person had their jobseeker support not expired.
Subclause (3) does not limit any review under section 304 of the Act.
Payment of jobseeker support and assistance granted under this regulation is subject to the condition that the person is liable to repay the whole of any amount paid for the relevant period if the person’s jobseeker support is not regranted under regulation 190L or 190M.
Any amount the person is liable to repay under this regulation is a debt due to the Crown and subject to recovery under section 362(1) of the Act (MSD’s duty to recover debts) by MSD from the person.
190O When reapplication form meets application form requirements
Regulations 190J to 190M apply for the purposes of section 297 of the Act.
190P When reapplication form treated as new application form
This regulation applies to a person if the requirements of regulation 190J(1)﻿(a) to (c)—
are not completed (by the person and by any included spouse or partner) within 20 working days starting on the expiry date for the person’s jobseeker support; or
were completed (by the person and by any included spouse or partner) within that 20-working-day period, and MSD is satisfied that there were no exceptional circumstances that prevented those requirements from being completed (by the person and by any included spouse or partner) within the period allowed to complete the requirements.
Jobseeker support must not be regranted to the person.
Any reapplication form received from or on behalf of the person is treated as an application form for the purposes of section 297(1) of the Act and regulation 166.
 Subpart 4 — Apportionment and payments on death of beneficiary
191 Apportionment
This regulation applies to, and enables MSD to apportion between a beneficiary and the beneficiary’s spouse or partner, the following (other than benefits specified in section 442(3) of the Act):
a benefit payable at a rate prescribed for a person who is in a relationship (a couple rate):
any other benefit payable under the Act.
Benefits to which this regulation applies are payable as follows (unless they are apportioned differently by MSD under subclause (3)):
50% must be paid to the beneficiary; and
50% must be paid to the spouse or partner who qualifies the beneficiary to be paid at the couple rate.
If MSD determines that it would not be appropriate for benefits to which this regulation applies to be paid under subclause (2), they are payable—
wholly to the beneficiary (and so not at all to the spouse or partner who qualifies the beneficiary to be paid at the couple rate), if MSD determines that is appropriate; or
in any other unequal proportions MSD determines are appropriate, to the beneficiary and to the spouse or partner who qualifies the beneficiary to be paid at the couple rate.
When a benefit is apportioned under this regulation, the proportion paid to a person is for the purposes of the Income Tax Act 2007 income of that person.
192 Payment on death of beneficiary
This subclause applies if,—
on the death of a beneficiary, an amount of the benefit concerned (which may include an instalment already due that has not been paid and remains payable) is unpaid; and
the amount is not disposed of under section 346 of the Act (for example, to or for the benefit of the surviving spouse or partner, or a dependent child or children, of the beneficiary).
If subclause (1) applies,—
if probate of the beneficiary’s will has been granted, or letters of administration of the beneficiary’s estate have been issued, the amount must be paid to the beneficiary’s executor or administrator:
in any other case, the amount must be paid in accordance with section 65(2) and (3) of the Administration Act 1969.
 Subpart 4A — Money management
192A Required manner of payment: money management for certain payments to people subject to sanction for first failure
This regulation applies—
to a main benefit of a person to whom section 236A applies (P); and
while money management imposed on P has effect.
In particular, this regulation applies to P’s main benefit (without limiting the generality of subclause (1)) while both of the following apply:
P’s main benefit is suspended for reasons unrelated to a sanction for failure to comply with 1 or more obligations under the Act that are specified in section 233 of the Act:
money management imposed on P has effect.
The prescribed proportion of an instalment of P’s main benefit, and that is an instalment payable to P (see regulation 163A(1)), must be paid by crediting that amount to a payment card for 4 weeks to enable P to obtain goods or services—
that MSD considers are essential; and
from participating suppliers.
A participating supplier, for the purposes of this section, means a supplier who has agreed with MSD to supply goods or services—
that MSD considers are essential; and
to a person on whom money management has been imposed; and
on the person’s presentation of the payment card.
Arrears that MSD must pay to P, and that are arrears that relate to a period while money management imposed on P has effect (even if the arrears are unrelated to P’s main benefit, but excluding arrears relating to a suspension to which subclause (2) applies), are—
paid to, or on account of, P personally under section 339(1) of the Act; and
not credited to P’s payment card.
Arrears that MSD must pay to P, and that are arrears that relate to a period while money management imposed on P has effect (even if the arrears are unrelated to P’s main benefit), and that are arrears relating to a suspension to which subclause (2) applies, are paid as follows:
the prescribed proportion of them is credited to P’s payment card:
the rest of them is paid to, or on account of, P personally under section 339(1) of the Act.
However, if P dies while money management imposed on P has effect, arrears to which subclause (6) applies must (despite that subclause) be paid under—
section 346 of the Act (required manner of payment: payment on death of beneficiary); or
regulation 192 (payment on death of beneficiary).
Debt that MSD must recover from P, and that is debt that relates to a period while money management imposed on P has effect (even if the debt is unrelated to P’s main benefit, and even if it is debt relating to a suspension to which subclause (2) applies), is recovered—
from, or on account of, P personally; and
not from, or from amounts to be credited to, P’s payment card.
However, if P dies while money management imposed on P has effect, the prescribed proportion of an instalment of P’s main benefit that is an instalment payable to P must (despite the rest of this regulation) be paid under—
section 346 of the Act (required manner of payment: payment on death of beneficiary); or
regulation 192 (payment on death of beneficiary).
 Subpart 5 — Youth money management
193 When youth money management applies
Regulation 194 applies to a young person (subject to subclause (2)) if—
a specified payment (as defined in subclause (3)) is payable to a young person; and
the young person has given to MSD or to the young person’s contracted service provider the information required under all or any of section 162(1)﻿(i) of the Act and regulations 166 and 173; and
the young person has not opted out under regulation 196.
Until the information required under all or any of section 162(1)﻿(i) of the Act and regulations 166 and 173 has been given, a payment referred to in subclause (1)﻿(a) must be paid to or on account of the young person entitled to it in accordance with section 339(1) of the Act.
In this subpart, specified payment, in relation to a young person, means,—
in accordance with section 341(3)﻿(a) to (c) of the Act,—
any youth support payment (including the in-hand allowance up to the maximum amount set out in clause 7 of Part 6 of Schedule 4 of the Act):
any WFF tax credit payable to a young person by MSD:
the amount of any child disability allowance or disability allowance to which a young person is entitled:
for the purposes of section 341(3)﻿(d) of the Act,—
the amount of any accommodation supplement, temporary additional support, or childcare assistance to which the young person is entitled (other than a payment for childcare assistance made to a provider under subpart 6 (childcare assistance) of Part 2 (assistance) of these regulations):
the amount of any special assistance of an ongoing kind under section 101 of the Act to which the young person is entitled (other than a payment for childcare made to a provider under any such welfare programme):
the amount of any incentive payment (as defined in Schedule 2 of the Act) payable to the young person:
in relation to a young person to whom section 166(1) or 167(1) of the Act applies, the portion of the specified benefit payable to the young person under regulation 191 (apportionment):
the amount of any winter energy payment payable to the young person.
194 How specified payment may be paid
MSD must pay each instalment of a specified payment payable to the young person in the following order and manner:
first, by paying such amount as MSD thinks reasonable in the circumstances of the young person directly towards the young person’s accommodation costs and service costs:
secondly, by paying such amount as MSD thinks reasonable in the circumstances of the young person directly towards the young person’s debts or other liabilities (including any reduction or deduction required or authorised under the Act):
thirdly, by paying to or on account of the young person the in-hand allowance up to the amount specified in clause 7 of Part 6 of Schedule 4 of the Act:
fourthly, unless subclause (2) applies, by crediting such amount as MSD thinks reasonable in the circumstances of the young person to a payment card, voucher, or device that enables the young person to obtain goods or services from a participating supplier.
Subclause (1) is subject to regulation 195 and section 282(3)﻿(c) of the Act.
In the case of the supply of goods or services to a young person by a non-participating supplier, MSD may pay the amount that would have been paid under subclause (1)﻿(d) either—
directly to that supplier; or
to or on account of the young person.
In this regulation, participating supplier means a supplier who has agreed with the department (as defined in section 3(1) of the Social Security Act 1964) or MSD to supply goods or services to young persons on presentation of a payment card, voucher, or other device, and non-participating supplier means a supplier who has not so agreed.
195 Payments that may be paid to young person or on account of young person
MSD may pay all or part of the following payments to or on account of a young person to whom regulation 194 applies:
any disability allowance or child disability allowance:
any special assistance under section 101(1) of the Act that is not of an ongoing kind:
any payments under the course participation assistance programme established under section 101(1) of the Act:
any flexible childcare assistance under the Employment and Work Readiness Assistance Programme:
any payment under the Training Incentive Allowance Programme:
any advance payment of all or part of 1 or more instalments of a benefit under section 347 of the Act.
Subclause (1) is subject to subclause (3).
MSD may only make a payment under subclause (1)﻿(b) to (d) after first consulting the young person’s contracted service provider, if there is one.
196 When young person may opt out of youth money management
A young person receiving a youth payment may opt out of youth money management if—
the young person is 18 years or older; and
the payment is being continued under section 54(3) of the Act.
A young person receiving a young parent payment may opt out of youth money management if—
the young person is 20 years or older; and
the payment is being continued under section 61(3) of the Act.
A young person opts out of youth money management by giving MSD written notice that the young person opts out of youth money management.
Regulation 194 does not apply to a young person who has opted out of youth money management under this regulation.
197 Criteria for managing payments
The criteria referred to in section 342 of the Act for the management of a young person’s own payments are as follows:
the young person has demonstrated continued compliance with the young person’s obligations under sections 162 to 164 of the Act; and
the young person’s contracted service provider has advised MSD that it considers the young person to be financially competent or, as the case requires, MSD considers the young person to be financially competent.
198 Young person who opts out of youth money management or meets criteria for managing own payments may request payments redirected
MSD, on the request of a young person who has opted out of youth money management under regulation 196 or has met the criteria set out in regulation 197 for managing the young person’s own payments, may pay all or part of a specified payment payable to the young person directly towards—
the young person’s accommodation and service costs:
the young person’s lawful debts or other liabilities (including any reduction or deduction required or authorised under the Act).
A request under subclause (1)—
must be in writing; and
must specify the costs, debts, or other liabilities towards which MSD must pay the payment; and
may be withdrawn or otherwise cancelled at any time.
199 Electing youth money management
A young person to whom section 166 or 167 of the Act applies for the time being may elect youth money management by giving MSD written notice that the young person elects youth money management.
200 Payment of specified payments if young person has elected youth money management
MSD must pay each instalment of a specified payment payable to a young person who has elected youth money management under regulation 199 in the following order and manner:
first, by paying such amount as MSD thinks reasonable in the circumstances of the young person directly towards the young person’s accommodation costs and service costs:
secondly, by paying such amount as MSD thinks reasonable in the circumstances of the young person directly towards the young person’s debts or other liabilities (including any reduction or deduction required or authorised under the Act):
thirdly, by paying the balance (if any, after payments under paragraphs (a) and (b)) to or on account of the young person.
 Subpart 6 — Advance payment of benefits
201 Interpretation
In this subpart, unless the context otherwise requires,—
applicable payment means any of the following:
an advance payment of all or part of 1 or more instalments of a benefit under section 347 of the Act:
a payment under the Special Needs Grants Programme (excluding Parts 5, 6, and 6A) established and approved on 18 December 1998 (and continued under clause 21 of Schedule 1 of the Act):
a payment under the Recoverable Assistance Programme (excluding Part 3A) established and approved on 15 February 1999 (and continued under clause 21 of Schedule 1 of the Act)
applicant means—
a beneficiary who wishes to make an application:
a beneficiary by whom or on whose behalf an application is made
application means an application for an advance payment of all or part of 1 or more instalments of a benefit under section 347 of the Act
budgeting activity means a budgeting activity of a kind prescribed in regulation 204.
202 Requirements for application
An applicant and the applicant’s spouse or partner (if any) must,—
if required by MSD, complete an application form provided by MSD; and
provide any information, or give an answer to any question, that MSD reasonably requires to determine the applicant’s application.
MSD may require an applicant and the applicant’s spouse or partner (if any) to comply with subclause (1)﻿(b)—
in the manner specified by MSD; and
whether or not they are required to complete the application form referred to in subclause (1)﻿(a).
203 Circumstances when budgeting activity may be required
For the purposes of section 348 of the Act, the circumstances in which an applicant or the applicant’s spouse or partner (if any), or both, may be required to undertake a budgeting activity are as follows:
if the applicant has received 3 or more applicable payments within—
the period that starts on the date of commencement of these regulations and ends on the date on which the application is made, if that period is less than 12 months; or
the preceding period of 12 months that ends on the date on which the application is made, if that date is 12 months or more after the date of commencement of these regulations; or
if paragraph (a) does not apply to the applicant, but MSD, after taking into account the following matters, considers that it would be beneficial for the applicant or the applicant’s spouse or partner (if any), or both, to undertake a budgeting activity to better manage their finances:
the applicant has received repeated applicable payments for the same cost (whether the payments were received before or after the date of commencement of these regulations):
the applicant’s history of receiving multiple applicable payments (whether that history relates to a period before or after the date of commencement of these regulations):
the amount of the applicant’s debts that are due to the Crown (within the meaning of section 353 of the Act) and whether that amount is materially significant to the management of the applicant’s finances.
If 1 or more applicable payments are made to the applicant on the same day, those payments are to be counted as 1 applicable payment under this regulation.
204 Kinds of budgeting activities
For the purposes of section 348 of the Act, the kinds of budgeting activities that MSD may require an applicant or the applicant’s spouse or partner (if any), or both, to undertake are as follows:
completing a budgeting work sheet or a work sheet approved by MSD that sets out income against expenditure:
attending a budgeting course or seminar approved by MSD that teaches how to prepare a budget or to improve budgeting skills:
seeking assistance from a recognised budgeting advice service.
 Subpart 7 — Calculating weekly income
205 Appropriate number of weeks for calculating weekly income
For the purposes of the definition of appropriate number of weeks in clause 11 of Part 3 of Schedule 3 of the Act and a benefit, the number of weeks is the appropriate number of weeks specified for that benefit in Schedule 6 of these regulations.
Subclause (1) does not prevent MSD, with the agreement of a person whose income would otherwise be determined (under clause 13 of Part 3 of Schedule 3 of the Act) over 52 weeks, determining that person’s income over the period of 1 week.
An agreement with a person under subclause (2) may be ended by the person or by MSD at any time by notice to the other.
 Subpart 8 — Debts
206 Debts due to the Crown
The following sums are debts due to the Crown:
a penalty payable under the Act:
Examples
A penalty payable under section 354 of the Act (recovery of penalty from beneficiary who obtains by fraud amount in excess of entitlement).
A penalty payable under regulation 226 (payer penalty for late deductions).
a benefit paid conditionally or provisionally under the Act that a person has (by direction of MSD or otherwise) become liable to repay:
Examples
An amount directed to be repayable under section 228(2) of the Act (MSD’s options in case of misconduct).
An amount the person granted a benefit is liable to repay under section 64 of the Act (emergency benefit: grant during epidemic in New Zealand) and to which this paragraph applies in accordance with section 64(3) of the Act.
An amount recoverable under section 186 of the Act (benefit may be subject to repayment of insurance payment).
An amount recoverable under regulation 184 (delayed redundancy and retirement payments).
An amount recoverable under regulation 185 (seasonal workers made redundant after benefit commences).
An amount recoverable under regulation 190E(6) (conditional payment of specified benefit and other assistance).
An amount recoverable under regulation 190N(6) (conditional payment of jobseeker support and other assistance).
An amount the person participating in approved activities is liable to repay under section 324 of the Act (effect of participation in certain activities on non-entitlement period).
An amount repayable under clause 8 of Part 4 or 5 of Schedule 4 of the Act (holiday allowance paid before 25 December to a person not entitled to it on 25 December).
An amount repayable under clause 13 of Part 4 or 5 of Schedule 4 of the Act (birthday allowance paid before the entitlement date to a person not entitled to it on that date).
an advance payment of all or part of 1 or more instalments of a benefit made to a person under section 347 of the Act:
any money paid to or for the credit of a person as a grant of special assistance under a programme approved under section 101 of the Act that is—
paid as a recoverable grant of assistance; or
otherwise recoverable from that person under the terms and conditions of the programme:
any amount described by the Act or these regulations as a debt due to the Crown from the person:
Examples
An amount recoverable under section 186 of the Act (benefit may be subject to repayment of insurance payment).
An amount recoverable under section 197(4)﻿(b) and (c) of the Act (effect of compensation or damages on application for benefit).
An amount recoverable under section 358 of the Act (recovery from spouse or partner who misleads MSD of excess amount beneficiary obtained).
a sum that is an overpayment, because the sum is paid or advanced under the Act, Part 6 of the Veterans’ Support Act 2014, Part 1 of the New Zealand Superannuation and Retirement Income Act 2001, or the Residential Care and Disability Support Services Act 2018, to or for the credit of a person, and the sum is one—
that is in excess of the amount to which the person is entitled; or
to which the person has no entitlement:
Examples
An amount recoverable under regulation 222 (deductions are held in trust).
An amount recoverable under regulation 226 (payer penalty for late deductions).
a debt that, under section 260(2), 266(2), or 384(2) of the Act, section 128(a) of the Public and Community Housing Management Act 1992, or any other enactment,—
may be recovered by MSD under regulations made under section 444 of the Act as if it were a debt due to the Crown; or
is a debt due to the Crown for the purposes of, or must be treated as if it were a debt due to the Crown under, such regulations.
 Subpart 9 — Recovery of debt
207 Exceptions to duty to recover debts
MSD’s duty under section 362 of the Act to recover debts is subject to—
regulation 208 (debts caused wholly or partly by errors to which debtors did not intentionally contribute):
regulation 208A (debts caused by disruption to automated electronic system):
regulation 208B (debts caused by specified errors identified through information share child support payment reviews):
subpart 11 (debt recovery suspension) of this Part, or any other regulations (providing for remittance or suspension of debt) made under section 444 of the Act:
a determination (about debts that it is uneconomic to recover) made under subclause (2):
an authorisation (about debts that are to be written off) of the kind specified in subclause (3).
MSD may, for the purposes of subclause (1)﻿(c), make a written determination specifying any 1 or more debts, or any 1 or more classes, descriptions, or kinds of debts, that it is uneconomic to recover.
An authorisation referred to in subclause (1)﻿(d) is a written authorisation (for example, a delegation)—
given jointly by the Minister and by the Minister of Finance for public finance purposes generally; and
that specifies any 1 or more classes, descriptions, or kinds of debts as debts that are to be written off.
208 Debts caused wholly or partly by errors to which debtors did not intentionally contribute
MSD cannot recover under the Act a sum comprising that part of a debt that was caused wholly or partly by an error to which the debtor did not intentionally contribute if—
the debtor—
received that sum in good faith; and
changed the debtor’s position in the belief that the debtor was entitled to that sum and would not have to pay or repay that sum to MSD; and
it would be inequitable in all the circumstances, including the debtor’s financial circumstances, to permit recovery.
In this regulation, error—
means—
the provision of incorrect information by MSD:
any erroneous act or omission of MSD that occurs during an inquiry under section 298 of the Act:
any other erroneous act or omission of MSD; but
does not include the simple act of making a payment to which the recipient is not entitled if that act is not caused, wholly or partly, by any erroneous act or omission of MSD.
In subclause (2), MSD includes a contracted service provider whose action or inaction is under section 377(c) of the Act treated as if it were action or inaction by MSD.
208A Debts caused by disruption to automated electronic system
MSD cannot recover under the Act a sum comprising a debt, or that part of a debt, that was caused wholly or partly by a disruption to an automated electronic system.
In this regulation,—
automated electronic system means an automated electronic system used under subpart 5A of Part 6 of the Act
disruption, to an automated electronic system, includes, without limitation, a disruption to disclosure to MSD of all or any information used in or by the system.
208B Debts caused by specified errors identified through information share child support payment reviews
MSD cannot recover under the Act a sum comprising a debt, or that part of a debt, that was caused wholly or partly by a specified error identified through an information share child support payment review.
In this regulation,—
information share child support payment review means a review under section 304(1A) of the Act of whether a person’s information share child support payment is or was all or any of their weekly income
specified error means 1 or both of the following:
MSD has charged an amount of income that is not the correct amount (see section 304A(1)﻿(c) of the Act):
a person has been incorrectly not identified as the proper recipient of the payment (whether that non-identification was by an automated electronic system or a person) (see section 304A(1)﻿(d) of the Act).
209 MSD must determine rate and methods of debt recovery
MSD must, in performing its duty under section 362 of the Act to recover a debt, determine from time to time—
the rate of recovery; and
the method or methods of recovery to be used.
The methods of recovery that MSD may determine under subclause (1)﻿(b) are to be used are any of the following:
bring proceedings in the name of the chief executive:
deduct all or part of that debt from any amount payable to that person by MSD as a benefit or a student allowance:
for a debt referred to in regulation 206(d) (recoverable special assistance money under an approved welfare programme), deduct all or part of that debt from any payment of a grant of special assistance under a programme approved under section 101 of the Act:
issue a deduction notice under regulation 217.
MSD must, in making the determinations required by subclause (1), have regard to—
relevant considerations specified in debt recovery directions given under regulation 211; and
all other relevant considerations.
Despite subclauses (1)﻿(a) and (3), the rate of recovery of a debt that is an emergency housing contribution must be one that is not inconsistent with the provisions of the Emergency Housing Grants Programme (as those terms are defined in regulation 3(1)) about recovery of a debt of that kind.
210 MSD may defer temporarily recovery of debt
This regulation applies to MSD when it is performing its duty under section 362 of the Act to recover a debt.
MSD may defer temporarily recovery of the debt if MSD is satisfied that, in the particular case, there exist circumstances of a kind specified by debt recovery directions given under regulation 211.
However, nothing in this regulation enables MSD to defer temporarily recovery of a debt that is an emergency housing contribution.
211 Debt recovery directions
The Minister must give to MSD, and ensure that there are in force (as from time to time amended or replaced) at all times on and after 26 November 2018, debt recovery directions for the purposes of regulations 209 and 210.
Debt recovery directions given under this regulation may, without limitation,—
specify for regulation 209 considerations that differ, or that contemplate different rates of recovery, or methods of recovery, or both, for determinations in respect of different kinds of debts referred to in regulation 206:
specify for regulation 210 different kinds of circumstances for different kinds of debts referred to in regulation 206.
Debt recovery directions given under this regulation are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
212 Recovery from estate of deceased beneficiary of excess amount beneficiary obtained
This regulation applies to the estate of a deceased beneficiary (A) if, on or after A’s death, MSD finds A to have been disqualified for any reason from obtaining the benefit, or a benefit of the amount, granted to A.
Example
A is disqualified because A received income, possessed property, or both.
An amount, to be assessed by MSD, equal to the total amount paid to A in excess of the amount (if any) to which A was by law entitled is a debt due to the Crown from, and may be recovered in proceedings brought by or on behalf of the chief executive against, A’s estate.
Recovery of the amount is subject to regulation 208 (but to nothing else in regulations 207 to 211).
213 Recovery from estate of beneficiary’s deceased spouse or partner of excess amount beneficiary obtained
This regulation applies to the estate of a deceased spouse or partner (B) of a beneficiary (A) if, on or after B’s death, MSD finds A to have been granted for any reason a benefit, or a benefit of an amount, to which A was not by law entitled.
Example
A was granted a benefit, or a benefit of an amount, to which A was not entitled because B, during B’s lifetime, received income, possessed property, or both.
An amount, to be assessed by MSD, equal to the total amount paid to A in excess of the amount (if any) to which A was by law entitled is a debt due to the Crown from, and may be recovered in proceedings brought by or on behalf of the chief executive against, B’s estate.
Recovery of the amount is subject to regulation 208 (but to nothing else in regulations 207 to 211).
214 Making and notice of assessment of amount
In determining under regulation 212 or 213 the total amount paid to A by way of benefit in excess of the amount (if any) to which A was by law entitled, any property that, when A or B died, was in A’s or B’s possession must be taken to have been in A’s or B’s possession on a date that MSD determines and that is, or is after, the date on which the benefit was first granted.
Subclause (1) is overridden to the extent that the contrary is—
proved in proceedings for the recovery of that excess amount; or
decided by the District Court on an application under regulation 215(1).
Written notice of an assessment under regulation 212 or 213 must be served on the executor or administrator of the estate.
215 Review of assessment of amount
An executor or administrator of an estate on whom a notice is served under regulation 214(3) may, within 1 month after the service, apply to the District Court to review 1 or both of the following:
the assessment under regulation 212 or 213:
any decision of MSD under regulation 214(1) in relation to the assessment.
The District Court has jurisdiction in respect of the application irrespective of the amount of the assessment.
The application must not be heard in open court.
The court may receive as evidence any statement, document, information, or matter that may in its opinion assist the court to deal effectively with the application, whether or not the same would be otherwise admissible in a court of law.
In determining the application, the court must accept a date determined under regulation 214(1) by MSD as the date on which any property was in the possession of the deceased, unless the court is satisfied from the evidence produced that some other date should be fixed.
In determining the application, the court may—
confirm or cancel, or increase or reduce the amount of, the assessment; or
make any order it considers just and equitable in the circumstances.
 Subpart 10 — Deductions
216 Amount due, etc, defined
In this subpart,—
amount due, for a person, means a debt—
that is an eligible debt due to the Crown, and that is subject to recovery or that may be recovered, from the person; and
notice of which is delivered or posted by MSD to (whether or not the notice is received by) the person; and
that is not paid by the end of the tenth day after the day on which MSD delivers or posts to the person the notice of the debt
debtor means the person (or person’s estate) from whom the amount due may be recovered as a debt due to the Crown
debtor’s payer has the meaning given to it by Schedule 2 of the Act
eligible debt due to the Crown, for a person, means a debt that—
is due to the Crown under regulation 206, and subject to recovery under section 362(1) of the Act, from the person; or
is recoverable, from the person’s estate, under regulation 212(2) (recovery from estate of deceased beneficiary of excess amount beneficiary obtained); or
is recoverable, from the person’s estate, under regulation 213(2) (recovery from estate of beneficiary’s deceased spouse or partner of excess amount beneficiary obtained); or
may be recovered from the person as a debt due to the Crown under section 27X of the Social Security Act 1964 (as that section is saved by section 256(1) of the Child Support Act 1991); or
is an amount of maintenance debt within the meaning of section 61CA of the Social Security Act 1964 (as that section is saved by clause 49 of Schedule 1 of the Act) that may be recovered from the person
salary or wages includes commission, and any other remuneration from employment.
217 Deduction notice requiring debtor’s payer to deduct, and pay MSD, amount due
MSD may issue to a debtor’s payer a written deduction notice that requires the debtor’s payer to—
deduct the amount due (as a lump sum, or by instalments) from a sum that is or becomes payable by the debtor’s payer to the debtor; and
pay to MSD by a time, or by any times, specified in the notice, the amount or amounts deducted.
Example
MSD may issue a written deduction notice to the Accident Compensation Corporation that requires the Corporation to—
deduct the amount due from any weekly compensation in respect of loss of earnings or loss of potential earning capacity that is payable, or becomes payable, to the debtor by the Corporation under the Accident Compensation Act 2001; and
pay to MSD at a time, or at any times, specified in the notice the amount or amounts deducted.
MSD cannot issue a deduction notice unless MSD has first taken other reasonably practicable steps (but this subclause does not require MSD to take court or tribunal proceedings) to recover the debt.
Every deduction notice operates subject to regulations 224 (protected earnings), 225 (variation or discharge), and 226 (payer penalty for late deductions).
218 Contents, copies, and revocation of deduction notice
MSD must specify in the deduction notice—
whether the deduction is to be made as a lump sum or by instalments; and
the time or times by which the debtor’s payer to whom the deduction notice is issued must pay to MSD the amounts deducted; and
the date, on or after the date on which the deduction notice is issued, on which the deduction notice takes effect.
MSD must issue a copy of the deduction notice to the debtor at the debtor’s last known place of residence or business.
MSD may at any time revoke a deduction notice issued to a debtor’s payer by giving written notice, or by issuing a new deduction notice, to the debtor’s payer.
MSD must revoke a deduction notice if satisfied, on a request for the purpose made by or on behalf of the debtor, that the amount due has been paid.
219 Debtor’s payer must, if requested, issue debtor deductions statement
Every debtor’s payer to whom a deduction notice is issued must, if requested by or on behalf of the debtor, issue to the debtor a written statement of—
each amount deducted; and
the purpose or purposes for which the debtor’s payer deducted each amount.
220 Debtor is, so far as amounts are deducted, discharged from debt
This regulation applies if a debtor’s payer to whom a deduction notice has been issued deducts, under the notice, any money payable to a debtor.
The debtor is, to the extent of the amount deducted, discharged from the debtor’s debt due to the Crown.
221 Deduction notices issued to banks
If the debtor’s payer to whom the deduction notice is issued is a bank, any money held by the bank to the credit of the debtor is subject to regulation 217, and the amount required to be deducted under the deduction notice is, without prejudice to any other remedies against the debtor or any other person,—
deemed to be held in trust for the Crown; and
recoverable from the bank under regulation 206(f) and section 362 of the Act as if it were money payable under a benefit to which the debtor was not entitled.
In this regulation, bank means any of the following:
a registered bank (within the meaning of the Banking (Prudential Supervision) Act 1989):
any other person carrying on in New Zealand the business of banking:
a private savings bank:
a credit union within the meaning of the Friendly Societies and Credit Unions Act 1982:
a building society within the meaning of the Building Societies Act 1965:
the Reserve Bank of New Zealand continued under the Reserve Bank of New Zealand Act 2021, but only in relation to an account maintained by that bank for an employee of that bank.
In this regulation, money held by the bank to the credit of the debtor includes interest on any money that is on deposit or deposited with a bank to the credit of the debtor, whether or not—
the deposit or depositing is on current account:
the money is to be at interest at a fixed term or without limitation of time:
the debtor has made any application to withdraw or uplift the money.
Money on deposit with a bank is, for the purposes of this regulation, taken to be held by the bank to the credit of the debtor if the money—
is held in a joint bank account in the name of the debtor and 1 or more other persons; and
can be withdrawn from the account by or on behalf of the debtor without a signature being required at the time of that withdrawal from, or on behalf of, the other person or persons.
222 Deductions are held in trust
A debtor’s payer who makes a deduction under a deduction notice is taken to be acting—
on the authority of the debtor and of any other person concerned; and
on behalf of the Crown.
Neither the debtor, nor any other person concerned, has a claim against the debtor’s payer making the deduction, or the Crown, in respect of that deduction.
Any amounts deducted must be held in trust for the Crown, and are debts due to the Crown under regulation 206(f), and subject to recovery under section 362 of the Act, from the debtor’s payer who makes the deductions, as if the amounts were money payable under a benefit to which the debtor’s payer who made the deduction was not entitled.
Any holding and recovery under subclause (3) is without prejudice to any other remedies against the debtor or any other person.
223 Offences in relation to deduction notices
A person commits an offence, and is liable on conviction to a fine not exceeding $2,000, if the person—
fails to make any deduction required by a deduction notice; or
fails, after making a deduction, to pay the amount deducted to MSD within the time specified in the notice; or
permits payment to or on behalf of any person, other than MSD, of any amount held in trust for the Crown under regulation 221 or 222.
224 Protected earnings
If a deduction notice is issued to an employer of a debtor, the employer must not, in making deductions under the deduction notice, reduce the amount paid to the debtor by way of salary or wages in respect of any week to an amount that is less than 60% of the amount calculated as being the debtor’s net ordinary weekly pay for a week.
The debtor’s net ordinary weekly pay for a week, for the purposes of this regulation, is the balance left after deducting from the debtor’s ordinary weekly pay (as defined in section 8 of the Holidays Act 2003) the amount of tax required to be withheld or deducted in accordance with the PAYE rules of the Income Tax Act 2007 if that ordinary weekly pay were the only salary or wages paid to the debtor by the employer in respect of a week.
This regulation overrides anything to the contrary in regulations 216 to 223, 225, and 226.
225 Variation or discharge of deduction notice
This regulation applies to a debtor’s payer to whom a deduction notice is issued, or a debtor, who thinks a deduction notice has been issued in error or contains an error.
The debtor’s payer or debtor may bring the matter to the notice of MSD.
If the matter is not resolved to the debtor’s payer’s or debtor’s satisfaction within 5 working days after the day on which the debtor’s payer or debtor brings it to the notice of MSD, the debtor’s payer or debtor may apply (without notice to MSD) to a Registrar of the District Court for the variation or discharge of the notice.
The Registrar may vary or discharge the notice if satisfied that an error has been made, and that the notice ought to be varied or discharged.
The variation or discharge takes effect when notice of it is given under regulation 238 to the debtor’s payer or debtor.
The Registrar must immediately send MSD a copy of the variation or discharge of the notice.
226 Penalty for late deductions
This regulation applies to a debtor’s payer to whom a deduction notice has been issued if the debtor’s payer fails wholly or in part to—
make a deduction required to be made by the notice; or
pay an amount deducted under the notice to MSD by the time specified in the notice.
The debtor’s payer is liable to pay to MSD a penalty calculated as follows:
on the amount in default, 10% of that amount or $5, whichever is the greater:
for each additional month or part of a month during which the amount in default or a part of it has not been deducted or, as the case may be, has not been paid to MSD, a further penalty of 2% of the amount or part in default, or $1, whichever is the greater.
MSD may remit all or part of the penalty payable, or may refund all or part of the penalty paid, to MSD under this regulation if satisfied—
that the failure to make the deduction or make the payment was due to circumstances reasonably beyond the debtor’s payer’s control; or
that the imposition of that penalty would in all circumstances be inequitable.
A penalty payable to MSD under this regulation is a debt due to the Crown for the purpose of regulation 206(f), and subject to recovery under section 362 of the Act, from the debtor’s payer as if the penalty were money payable under a benefit to which the debtor’s payer was not entitled.
 Subpart 11 — Debt recovery suspension
227 Interpretation
In this subpart, unless the context otherwise requires,—
benefit debt means a debt due to the Crown (other than a debt that is an emergency housing contribution), and subject to recovery, under section 362(1) (MSD’s duty to recover debts) of the Act
cash assets has the same meaning as in section 68(2) of the Act
deduction notice means a deduction notice issued under regulation 217
full-time course has the same meaning as in section 2(1) of the Student Allowances Regulations 1998
holiday employment means employment undertaken by a full-time student—
after the end of a full-time course in which the student was enrolled in one academic year and before the start of a full-time course in which the student is, or is to be, enrolled in the next academic year; or
after the end of a full-time course in which the student was enrolled in one academic year and before the start of another full-time course in which the student is, or is to be, enrolled in that year; or
after the end of a portion of a full-time course in which the student is enrolled in one academic year and before the resumption in that year of the rest of that course
notice revoking the deduction notice, in relation to a deduction notice, means a written notice—
given by MSD in accordance with regulation 218(3); and
revoking the deduction notice
period of suspension has the meaning given to it by regulation 233
permanent employment position means a position of employment that is—
full employment or full-time employment; but
not temporary employment
person eligible to request suspension has the meaning given to it by regulation 228
request for suspension has the meaning given to it in regulation 231
suspension deadline, in relation to a person eligible to request suspension, means the close of the day that is 3 months after the date on which the person commences employment in a permanent employment position
working-age benefit means a benefit that is—
jobseeker support; or
sole parent support; or
an emergency benefit (other than one payable under section 17(2)﻿(c) of the New Zealand Superannuation and Retirement Income Act 2001); or
a youth payment; or
a young parent payment; or
a supported living payment.
 Eligibility to request debt recovery suspension
228 Person eligible to request suspension defined
A person eligible to request suspension means a person—
who is, or is treated as, a person who complies with regulation 229 (which specifies work status and associated requirements); and
who complies with regulation 230 (which specifies cash assets, income, and associated requirements).
229 Work status and associated requirements
A person complies with this regulation if—
the person is 18 years old or older; and
MSD is satisfied that the person has entered, or is to enter, into a permanent employment position; and
the person complies with subclause (2).
A person complies with this subclause if—
the person is, or is treated as, a person receiving a working-age benefit; or
the person has been granted a working-age benefit that, under section 313 of the Act, is subject to a stand down and commences on a date calculated in accordance with section 316 of the Act; or
the person is a full-time student who is not undertaking holiday employment; or
the person—
is not, and is not treated as, a person receiving a working-age benefit; and
has moved or is moving from a permanent employment position or position of employment of some other kind (the first position) to a permanent employment position (the second position); and
has commenced or is to commence employment in the second position after (but not more than 4 weeks after) the person’s employment in the first position terminated or is to terminate; or
the person—
is not, and is not treated as, a person receiving a working-age benefit; and
is a person who MSD is satisfied is re-entering or has re-entered the workforce.
MSD may for the purposes of these regulations treat a person younger than 18 years as a person who complies with this regulation if—
the person complies with subclause (1)﻿(b) and (c); and
MSD considers there are special circumstances that warrant that treatment.
230 Cash assets, income, and associated requirements
A person complies with this regulation if MSD is satisfied that the person has entered, or is to enter, into a permanent employment position and—
the person has cash assets (including the cash assets of the person’s spouse or partner, if any) of less than,—
for a single person, the amount in clause 1 of Schedule 5 of the Act; or
for a person who is in a relationship (with or without dependent children), or is a sole parent (with 1 or more dependent children), the amount in clause 2 of Schedule 5 of the Act; and
the person has income (including the income of the person’s spouse or partner, if any) of less than the appropriate amount set out in Part 2 of Schedule 5 of the Act; and
if the person has been granted a working-age benefit that, under section 313 of the Act, is subject to a stand down and commences on a date calculated in accordance with section 316 of the Act, or is, or is treated as, a person receiving a working-age benefit, the person has taken all practicable steps to ensure that the grant of the benefit or, as the case may be, the benefit, is cancelled when the person commenced or is to commence employment in the permanent employment position.
231 Making and withdrawing requests for suspension
A person eligible to request suspension may, at any time before the suspension deadline,—
make a request that recovery of the person’s benefit debts be suspended (a request for suspension); or
withdraw a request for suspension the person made.
A request for suspension may be made orally or in writing to MSD but, if made orally, must as soon as practicable be confirmed in writing.
A withdrawal of a request for suspension must be made in writing to MSD.
A request for suspension, or withdrawal of one, must be treated as having been made when it is received by MSD.
A person is not prevented from making or withdrawing a request for suspension just because the person has, in accordance with this regulation,—
made 1 or more earlier requests for suspension; and
withdrawn those 1 or more earlier requests.
232 Suspension of recovery of benefit debts
This regulation applies to a person who—
is a person eligible to request suspension; and
has, in accordance with regulation 231, made a request for suspension; and
has not, in accordance with regulation 231, withdrawn the request.
Benefit debts of a person to whom this regulation applies must not, during the period of suspension, be recovered in any of the following ways:
by way of proceedings or deductions, and under subpart 10:
by way of the issuing of, or deductions under, a deduction notice.
As soon as practicable after this regulation starts to apply to a person, MSD must give a notice revoking the deduction notice in respect of every deduction notice that—
relates to benefit debts of the person; and
has not already been revoked or otherwise ceased to have effect.
MSD must take all practicable steps to ensure that a notice given in accordance with subclause (3) is received—
as soon as practicable, if the person has commenced employment in the permanent employment position when the request for suspension is made; and
as soon as practicable after the person commences employment in the permanent employment position, if the person has not commenced employment in that position when the request for suspension is made.
233 Period of suspension defined
The period of suspension means the period that—
starts (for amounts other than those referred to in paragraph (b)) at the later of the following times:
the time the relevant request to suspend recovery was made; and
the time the person commenced employment in the permanent employment position; and
starts for amounts to be recovered by way of deductions under a deduction notice at the time (determined in accordance with subpart 10) that the person to whom the deduction notice was issued received a notice revoking the deduction notice; and
ends on the suspension deadline.
 How receipt of working-age benefit affects debt recovery suspension
234 Suspension starts only if benefit has been cancelled
This regulation applies to a person described in regulation 232(1) who is, or is treated as, a person who is receiving, or has been granted, a working-age benefit.
Recovery of the person’s benefit debts must not be suspended in accordance with regulation 232 unless—
the working-age benefit or, as the case may be, the grant of that benefit, has been cancelled; or
the person is treated as a person who was receiving a working-age benefit that has now been cancelled.
This regulation overrides regulation 232.
235 Suspension ceases if person receives benefit
Suspension of the recovery of a person’s benefit debts in accordance with regulation 232 ceases if the person—
receives a working-age benefit; or
is treated as a person receiving a working-age benefit.
This regulation overrides regulation 232.
 Certain spouses or partners treated as persons who are or were receiving working-age benefits
236 Non-qualifying spouses or partners of recipients of New Zealand superannuation or veteran’s pension
This regulation applies to a person (person A) who is the spouse or partner of another person (person B) who is receiving, in person B’s own right, the whole or a portion of New Zealand superannuation or a veteran’s pension paid at a grandparented rate.
Person A must, unless subclause (3) applies, be treated for the purposes of this subpart as a person receiving a working-age benefit.
Person A must instead be treated for the purposes of this subpart as a person who was receiving a working-age benefit that has now been cancelled if—
person B elects to no longer receive New Zealand superannuation or a veteran’s pension paid at a grandparented rate (as referred to in clause 7(2)﻿(b) of Schedule 1AA of the New Zealand Superannuation and Retirement Income Act 2001); and
because of that election, the appropriate rate of benefit payable to person B is the whole or a portion of New Zealand superannuation or a veteran’s pension paid at a couple rate other than a grandparented rate.
In this regulation,—
couple rate means a rate prescribed for a person who is in a relationship
grandparented rate means—
a rate specified in clause 1 of Part 2 of Schedule 1 of the New Zealand Superannuation and Retirement Income Act 2001; or
the relationship (partner not receiving superannuation or pension) rate specified in regulations referred to in section 169 of the Veterans’ Support Act 2014.
237 Spouses or partners of persons granted working-age benefits payable at certain rates
This regulation applies to a person (person A) who is the spouse or partner of another person (person B) if person B has been granted a working-age benefit payable at the rate for a person whose spouse or partner has not been granted a benefit in the spouse’s or partner’s own right.
Person A must, unless subclause (3) applies, be treated for the purposes of this subpart as a person receiving a working-age benefit.
If person A commences employment in a permanent employment position and the rate of benefit payable is abated, on account of the income from the employment, to half, or less than half, of the rate referred to in subclause (1), person A must instead be treated for the purposes of this subpart as a person who was receiving a working-age benefit that has now been cancelled.
 Subpart 12 — Requirements to give notice or other document
238 How MSD or person can meet requirement to give written notice or other document
This regulation specifies ways in which MSD or a person can meet a requirement in the Act or these regulations to give to another person or MSD a written notice or other document.
The written notice or other document may be given to an individual (other than an officer or employee in the service of the Crown in that person’s official capacity)—
personally in hard copy form; or
by leaving it in hard copy form at that person’s usual or last known place of residence or business or at the address specified by that person in any application or other document received from that person; or
by posting it in hard copy form in a letter addressed to that person at that place of residence or business or at that address; or
by an electronic means (with the individual’s express or inferred consent) in accordance with Part 4 of the Contract and Commercial Law Act 2017.
The written notice or other document may be given to any other person, including an officer or employee in the service of the Crown in that person’s official capacity,—
if applicable, personally in hard copy form; or
by leaving it in hard copy form at that person’s place of business; or
by posting it in hard copy form in a letter addressed to that person at that place of business; or
by an electronic means (with the person’s express or inferred consent) in accordance with Part 4 of the Contract and Commercial Law Act 2017.
Written notices or other documents given by post are, in the absence of evidence to the contrary, taken to have been received by the person on the fourth day after the date on which the letter was posted (and, in proving postal delivery, it is enough to prove the letter was properly addressed and posted).
This regulation—
is subject to section 254 of the Act (how notice of sanction may be given); and
operates under sections 392(2), 400(2), and 412(2) of the Act.
 Subpart 13 — Medical services
239 Rates and conditions of employment and payment: prescribed health practitioners
A health practitioner who is any of the following, acting within their scope of practice, is prescribed for the purposes of section 379 of the Act:
a medical practitioner:
a psychologist:
a dentist:
a midwife:
a nurse practitioner.
 Subpart 14 — Reciprocity agreements: requirements and process
240 Requirements for agreements with mutual assistance provisions
A reciprocity agreement may contain and include mutual assistance provisions of 1 or both of the following kinds:
provisions for the Government of New Zealand and the Government of another country to provide each other with assistance in the recovery of social security debts:
provisions for those Governments to supply each other with information for social security purposes.
No mutual assistance provision of the kind referred to in subclause (1)﻿(a) may be contained and included in a reciprocity agreement unless the agreement is subject to—
the terms and conditions set out in regulation 241; or
terms and conditions that are to an equivalent effect.
No mutual assistance provision of the kind referred to in subclause (1)﻿(b) may be contained and included in a reciprocity agreement unless the agreement is subject to—
the terms and conditions set out in regulation 242; or
terms and conditions that are to an equivalent effect.
241 Agreement terms and conditions for debt recovery
The terms and conditions referred to in regulation 240(2)﻿(a) are as follows:
assistance to recover any social security debt of a party may be provided by the other party only in respect of a debt—
that has been found or determined to be owing in the country concerned by a court or tribunal having jurisdiction in the matter, or by a person, a body, or an organisation in that country acting administratively within the terms of a lawful authorisation; and
in respect of which any right of review of or appeal against the determination of the debt, under the law under which the debt was determined (other than a right of judicial review or complaint under laws relating to administrative decisions generally, or under human rights laws),—
has been exhausted or has expired; or
if there is no time limit for the exercise of any such right, has not been exercised, or has been exercised and the review or appeal has been finally determined; and
that may be lawfully recovered under the laws of that country; and
that was first found or determined to be owing less than 5 years before the date that the request for assistance is made, except as provided in subclause (3):
when providing assistance to recover any social security debt of a party, the party giving the assistance is not required to—
give priority to the recovery of social security debts of the other party; and
take any measures for recovery not provided for under the laws relating to the recovery of debts of that party:
a party cannot seek to recover a social security debt by imprisonment of the individual by whom it is owed or of any other individual:
any recovery of a social security debt of a deceased individual is limited to the value of that individual’s estate:
any institution, court, or tribunal involved in the recovery of a social security debt may defer recovery of the debt, or may order or arrange for the debt to be paid in instalments, if—
the institution, court, or tribunal has the power to do so; and
it is its normal practice to do so:
a party may give assistance only in respect of a social security debt that the requesting institution has certified is of a kind described in paragraph (a)﻿(i) to (iv):
if a party gives assistance in respect of a social security debt for which there is no time limit for exercising a right of review of or appeal against the determination of the debt under the law under which the debt was determined, or for which there is a time limit but a right of review or appeal may be exercised after its expiry in the discretion of the court or tribunal or body or person concerned, or in the discretion of any other person,—
the requesting institution must immediately advise the requested institution if a right of that kind is exercised; and
the requested institution must defer recovery of the debt until notified by the requesting institution that the review or appeal is finally determined:
the party requesting assistance to recover a social security debt must pay the costs of the other party of recovering the debt, including court costs or other fees payable under the laws of that party.
For the purposes of subclause (1)﻿(a)﻿(ii), a right of review or appeal under the law under which a debt was determined has expired—
if the right has not been exercised within the time limit provided for its exercise; and
irrespective of whether the court or tribunal or body or person that would have jurisdiction in respect of the review or appeal, or any other person, retains a discretion (however expressed in that law) to allow the right of review or appeal to be exercised after that time.
If an institution, a court, or a tribunal defers the recovery of a social security debt, or orders or arranges for a social security debt to be paid by instalments, the 5-year period referred to in subclause (1)﻿(a)﻿(iv) is extended by the period of deferral or, as the case requires, the period over which the debt is to be paid by instalments.
242 Agreement terms and conditions for information exchange
The terms and conditions referred to in regulation 240(3)﻿(a) are as follows:
a request for information relating to an individual may be made only for social security purposes:
the requested institution of a party may supply to the requesting institution any information about the person to whom the request relates that it holds or is lawfully able to obtain:
the requesting institution of a party (the requesting party) to whom information is supplied by the requested institution of the other party may supply that information to the taxation authorities of the requesting party for either or both of the following purposes:
making an assessment of the tax due by any person under the laws of the requesting party relating to taxation:
detecting tax fraud or tax evasion under the laws of the requesting party:
every request for and supply of information made by and to the competent institutions of the parties must be made in terms of an agreement between the competent institutions of the parties that—
specifies the types of information that the competent institutions may supply to each other; and
limits the supply of information to the types of information specified; and
in relation to New Zealand, contains, with all necessary modifications, the safeguards (subject to subparagraph (iv)) required to be set out in an information matching agreement within the meaning of section 178 of the Privacy Act 2020; and
in relation to New Zealand, requires the information matching rules set out in clause 3 of Schedule 6 of the Privacy Act 2020, with all necessary modifications, to be applied; and
in relation to New Zealand, has been agreed to by the Privacy Commissioner, the Commissioner having had regard to the matters in section 381(2)﻿(a) of the Act:
any information supplied by a party to the other party must be subject to the same privacy protections (subject to paragraphs (b) and (c)) as any other personal information obtained under the social security laws of the other party:
no party that receives, under the agreement, personal information about any individual from the competent institution of the other party may supply that information to any other country without the prior written consent of that competent institution or the individual concerned:
a party must supply the competent institution of the other party with any information required by that institution to answer any questions or to make any report or return required by a person or body authorised to monitor compliance with that party’s privacy laws.
In relation to New Zealand, section 178(3) of the Privacy Act 2020 applies, with any necessary modifications, to an agreement under subclause (1)﻿(d) between the competent institutions of the parties.
 Adverse action arising from discrepancy
243 Adverse action against individual if discrepancy shown by information from other country
This regulation applies if information received by MSD from the competent institution of another country under a mutual assistance provision of the kind referred to in regulation 240(1)﻿(b) has produced a discrepancy, and as a result MSD proposes to take an adverse action against an individual.
MSD must give that individual written notice—
specifying particulars of the discrepancy and of the adverse action MSD proposes to take; and
stating that the individual has 5 working days from the receipt of the notice in which to show cause why that action should not be taken.
MSD cannot take an adverse action against an individual to whom a notice has been sent until the expiry of the 5 working days referred to in subclause (2)﻿(b).
Subclauses (2) and (3) do not prevent MSD from taking adverse action against an individual if compliance with the requirements of subclause (2) would prejudice an investigation into the commission, or the possible commission, of an offence.
A written notice to be given under subclause (2) may be given as specified in regulations made under the Privacy Act 2020 for the giving of notices under section 181 of that Act.
A failure by MSD to comply, in relation to an individual, with subclause (2) is taken for the purposes of Part 5 of the Privacy Act 2020, to constitute a failure to comply with the provisions of subpart 4 of Part 7 of that Act.
In this regulation and regulation 244, expressions defined in section 177 of the Privacy Act 2020 have the meanings so defined, with any necessary modifications.
244 Exception if specified information exchange provision produces unique identifier discrepancy
This regulation applies if information received by MSD from the competent institution of another country under an information exchange provision to which subclause (4) applies—
contains identifying information about an individual (including the unique identifier assigned to that individual by that institution) who has applied for a benefit payable under the agreement in either country; and
produces a discrepancy with information already held by MSD about that person.
MSD must give that individual written notice (unless MSD has previously given notice under this subclause to that individual, whether for the discrepancy or another discrepancy)—
specifying particulars of the discrepancy and of the action MSD proposes to take as a result of that discrepancy; and
specifying the unique identifiers assigned to that individual by the competent institutions of both countries; and
informing that individual that information received from the other country about that individual will be matched with information in MSD’s possession using those unique identifiers; and
specifying the kinds of information about the individual that MSD is likely to receive from the other country under the agreement; and
specifying the kinds of actions that MSD may take as a result of information about that individual received at any later time from the other country under the agreement; and
specifying the likely consequences of those actions for any benefit payable to that individual under the agreement or otherwise; and
stating that the individual has 5 working days from the receipt of the notice to show cause why MSD should not take the action referred to in paragraph (a) or actions of the kinds referred to in paragraph (e).
After the 5 working days referred to in subclause (2)﻿(g), MSD need not comply with regulation 243(2) and (3) in respect of any information that is—
later received from the other country under the provision; and
received for a purpose set out in subclause (4); and
matched against information held by MSD using the unique identifiers assigned to the individual concerned by the competent institutions of both countries.
This subclause applies to a provision that is contained in a reciprocity agreement, and that is a provision of the kind referred to in regulation 240(1)﻿(b), but only if the social security purposes concerned are wholly or principally the administration of the agreement itself, and the parties to the agreement are likely often to exchange information about individuals for all or any of the following purposes:
granting or paying benefits under the agreement:
calculating the rates of benefits payable under the agreement:
calculating the rates of other benefits that are affected by the rates of benefits payable under the agreement.
 Part 7 — Reviews and appeals
245 Guide to Part 7
Part 7 deals with the following topics:
reviews by a benefits review committee:
appeals to an appeal authority:
appeals to the High Court by way of case stated:
appeals to the medical board.
 Reviews by benefits review committee
246 Application under section 391 of Act to be made in writing
An application for review under section 391 of the Act must be made in writing.
247 Procedure on review
The committee must, as soon as practicable after it receives an application for review, review the decision.
In reviewing the decision, the committee may, in accordance with the Act, decide to confirm, vary, or revoke the decision.
The committee must give written notice of its decision on the review to the applicant for review.
The written notice must include the reasons for the decision, and advice that the applicant has, under sections 395 to 399 of the Act, a right to appeal to the appeal authority against the decision if the committee has, under this regulation, confirmed or varied MSD’s decision.
 Appeals to appeal authority
248 Appeal is begun by written notice of appeal lodged within 3 months of notification or further allowed period
An appeal under section 397, 398, or 399 of the Act is begun by a written notice of appeal.
The notice of appeal must state with particularity the grounds of appeal and the relief sought.
The notice of appeal must be lodged with the Secretary of the appeal authority within the period allowed by section 400 of the Act.
The parties to an appeal to the appeal authority are—
the person to whom section 397, 398, or 399 of the Act gives the right to appeal against the decision (the appellant); and
MSD.
Before or immediately after the notice of appeal is lodged, the appellant must provide a copy of it to MSD.
249 Material that MSD must send to authority
MSD must, as soon as possible after receiving the copy of the notice of appeal, send to the Secretary of the appeal authority copies of the following:
any application, documents, written submissions, statements, reports, and other papers lodged with, received by, or prepared for, MSD and relating to the decision appealed against:
a copy of any notes made by or by direction of MSD of the evidence given at the hearing (if any) before MSD:
any exhibits in the custody of MSD:
a copy of the decision appealed against:
a report setting out the matters considered in making the decision.
The authority may direct that MSD send a further report to the Secretary of the appeal authority (as well as the report sent under subclause (1)﻿(e)).
MSD must give or send promptly to each other party to the appeal a copy of every report or further report sent under subclause (1)﻿(e) or (2).
Each other party to the appeal is entitled to be heard and to offer evidence on a matter referred to in a report or further report sent under subclause (1)﻿(e) or (2).
Subclause (4) is subject to regulation 250(3).
250 Fixing time and place for hearing of appeal
The appeal authority must, as soon as convenient after receiving a notice of appeal,—
fix a time and place for the hearing of the appeal; and
give the appellant and MSD a reasonable period of notice of the hearing.
The reasonable period of notice must be at least 10 days (excluding both the day of giving the notice and the day of the hearing).
Subclause (1) does not apply if the authority considers that the appeal can be properly determined without a hearing.
251 Expenses payable to appellants
This regulation applies to an appellant whom the appeal authority requests to appear before it if the appellant does so.
MSD must, out of money appropriated by Parliament for the purpose, pay the actual and reasonable travelling and accommodation expenses (if any) incurred by the appellant.
252 Hearing of appeal: representation
At the hearing of any appeal,—
MSD may be represented by counsel or by an MSD employee; and
any other party may appear and act personally or by counsel or any duly authorised representative.
253 Hearing of appeal: evidence
Every appeal to the appeal authority against a decision of MSD must be by way of rehearing; but if any question of fact is involved in any appeal, the relevant evidence taken before or received by MSD must, subject to any special order, be brought before the authority as follows:
in respect of any evidence given orally, by the production of a copy of the notes of MSD or of any other material the authority thinks expedient:
in respect of any evidence taken by affidavit and any exhibits, by the production of the affidavits and any exhibits that may have been sent to the authority by MSD, and by the production by the parties to the appeal of any exhibits in their custody.
Subclause (1) is subject to regulation 250(3).
On an appeal against a decision of MSD, despite subclause (1), the authority—
may rehear all or any part of the evidence; and
must rehear the evidence of a witness if the authority has reason to believe that any note made by MSD of the evidence of that witness is or may be incomplete in a material particular.
The authority has full discretionary power to hear and receive evidence or further evidence on questions of fact, whether by oral evidence, by affidavit, or by evidence given by other written means or by electronic means.
The authority must also have regard to any report lodged by MSD under regulation 249 and to any matters referred to in it and to any evidence offered on it, whether or not those matters would be otherwise admissible in evidence.
In the exercise of its powers under this regulation, the authority may receive as evidence any statement, document, information, or matter that, in the authority’s opinion, may assist it to deal with the matters before it, whether or not the same would be admissible in a court of law.
254 Determination of appeal
The authority may confirm, modify, or reverse the decision appealed against.
Subclause (1) enables the authority to exercise or perform only powers, duties, functions, and discretions that MSD had in respect of the same matter.
Despite subclause (1), the authority may refer to MSD for further consideration all or a part of the matter to which an appeal relates.
The authority must advise MSD of its reasons for the referral and give any directions it thinks just in respect of the rehearing or reconsideration or otherwise of all or a part of the matter referred.
255 Costs award or order in favour of, or against, appellant
If an appeal is allowed in whole or in part, or all or a part of the matter is referred back to MSD, the authority may allow the appellant the costs of bringing all or a part of the appeal.
If an appeal is not allowed, no award of costs can be made against the appellant unless, in the authority’s opinion, the appeal was frivolous or vexatious or ought not to have been brought.
The amount of costs awarded must be stated in the order.
If an award of costs is made under this regulation and any sum remains unpaid, the person in whose favour the award or order was made may obtain from the Secretary of the authority a certificate under seal of the sum awarded. If the certificate is filed in the District Court, it may be enforced in respect of the amount specified in it (if any) that is still owing as if it were a judgment of that court.
256 MSD may be required to pay authority’s costs
This regulation applies if an appeal is allowed in whole or in part and the authority has incurred costs in hearing and determining the appeal.
